市場焦點
美國
• Fed褐皮書顯示，美國經濟整體維持溫和擴張，10個地區經濟活動小幅至溫和成長，消費及企
業需求仍有支撐 ，但住宅市場偏弱 、消費者價格敏感度升高 。就業僅小幅增加 ，薪資溫和成
長；另一方面 ，能源、運輸、原物料及關稅成本持續推升企業支出 ，物價在多數地區溫和上
升，反映經濟韌性與通膨壓力並存，增加Fed政策判斷難度。
美伊衝突
• 美國８月底宣布對逾 60個伊朗相關實體實施制裁 ，試圖切斷伊朗的石油 、金融與貿易收入 。
但伊朗歷經數十年制裁後 ，已建立相對成熟的制裁規避網絡 ，但短期預期美國仍難迫使德黑
蘭屈服。對金融市場而言 ，若制裁未進一步衝擊伊朗石油出口 ，油價上行空間相對有限；反
之，若美國擴大次級制裁並波及中國等主要買家，則可能推升原油價格與全球通膨預期。

韓股
• 儘管市場波動劇烈 ，韓國企業在第⼆季度財報季的盈利前景仍在持續改善 ，在三星電⼦和 SK
海力⼠公佈業績後，MSCI韓國指數的全年每股純益預期被小幅上調 ，此前5至6月修正步伐一
度放緩，如今⼜重新加快，整體來看，今年韓國盈利預期累計上調幅度接近 190%，並推動整
個新興市場盈利預期提升約 37%，晶⽚需求指引依然強勁 ，隨著盈利預測持續上調 ，台灣市
場也從中受益。

美國製造業維持擴張，需求降溫與高成本壓力牽制擴張動能
• 新訂單降溫，製造業維持擴張韌性 ：美國8月ISM製造業PMI降至54.6%，較7月55.6%回落，但仍連續第8個月位於擴張
區間；新訂單降至 53.7%、訂單積壓降至 51.8%，顯示需求動能較前月放緩 ，惟生產指數仍達 58.3%，連續第10個月擴
張，運輸設備、機械及石油與煤炭等主要產業維持成長，製造業整體仍具韌性。
• 生產韌性延續，價格壓力仍偏高：就業指數降至51.2%，雖較前月走弱但仍處擴張區間；供應商交貨指數升至59.3%，代
表供應商交期進一步拉長 ，而價格指數維持71.1%，原物料價格已連續 23個月上升，鋼鋁、關稅及石化產品成本仍是主
要壓力來源。整體呈現「需求降溫、生產偏強、成本仍高」的格局，企業利潤與通膨傳導仍需觀察。

• 後市觀察：製造業景氣仍具韌性 ，但新訂單放緩與投入成本高檔並存 ，後續應觀察需求能否延續 ，以及鋼鋁、能源與關
稅成本是否進一步轉嫁至終端價格上 。投資布局可聚焦半導體 、電機電⼦及AI基礎建設等受惠於製造投資與 AI資本支出
的領域；債券則以中短天期及投資等級公司債為主，降低利率波動風險，以股債配置分散景氣與利率風險。
ISM製造業PMI關鍵分項指標比較
資料來源：ISM Manufacturing PMI Report ，2026.9.1
ISM製造業PMI月變動趨勢

褐皮書揭美國經濟溫和成長，成本壓力增添Fed政策難度
• 經濟溫和擴張，消費與企業需求仍有支撐：Fed褐皮書
顯示，自7月初至8月下旬，10個地區經濟活動小幅至
溫和成長、2個地區持平，消費支出整體小幅增加 ，製
造業、部分服務業及金融活動有所改善 ，資料中心與
國防相關需求亦提供支撐；但住宅市場表現仍偏弱 ，
消費者對價格更加敏感 ，顯示經濟維持韌性但內需表
現分化。
• 就業增幅有限 ，成本壓力仍牽動通膨 ：就業整體僅小
幅增加，7個地區就業成長 、5個地區大致持平 ，薪資
多數維持溫和上升；然而能源 、運輸、金屬、石化原
料及關稅成本仍持續推升企業投入成本 ，部分企業甚
至難以完全轉嫁成本，整體價格仍呈現上升。
• 市場觀察： 褐皮書反映美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但就

業降溫與通膨壓力並存，使Fed政策取向更具挑戰。近
期市場對9月升息的預期明顯升溫 ，政策仍高度依賴後
續通膨與就業數據 。建議債券配置宜以短中天期美債
及投資級公司債為主 ，降低長天期利率波動；股票則
聚焦大型優質科技 、AI基礎建設及高現金流企業 ，並
搭配黃金分散通膨與地緣政治風險。
資料來源：Fed Beige Book，兆豐銀行整理， 2026.9.3

制裁使伊朗經濟受創，仍難迫使政權屈服
上海合作組織(SCO)佔全球經濟(GDP)的四分之一
資料來源：IMF，Bloomberg，2026.9.1，其他上海合作組織成員國包括巴基斯坦、伊朗、哈薩克、烏茲別克、
白俄羅斯、蒙古、吉爾吉斯、塔吉克和阿富汗資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.8.26
伊朗絕大部分石油銷往中國
• 制裁聲勢強，但關鍵仍在中國：美國８月底宣布對逾60個伊朗相關實體實施制裁，試圖切斷伊朗的石油、金融與貿易收入。
制裁能否真正奏效，關鍵仍在於是否將中國納入主要打擊對象。伊朗近八成的石油銷往中國，美方現階段僅將少數中港企業
列入黑名單，刻意避開了中國大型國有金融與能源巨頭，若強行實施全面制裁，可能危及關鍵礦產供應、破壞脆弱的美中貿
易休兵，甚至推升全球能源價格。

• 制裁使伊朗經濟受創，仍難迫使政權屈服：伊朗歷經數十年制裁後，已建立相對成熟的制裁規避網絡，並透過中國、俄羅斯、
印度及中亞國家參與的上海合作組織（SCO）等非西方平台維持經貿往來。俄羅斯與伊朗合作持續深化，巴基斯坦亦表態無
義務遵守美方制裁，印度則在制裁壓力下維持一定程度的經貿交流。因此，美國新一輪制裁雖將加劇伊朗經濟壓力，但短期
仍難迫使德黑蘭屈服。對金融市場而言，若制裁未進一步衝擊伊朗石油出口，油價上行空間相對有限；反之，若美國擴大次
級制裁並波及中國等主要買家，則可能推升原油價格與全球通膨預期，進而延後聯準會降息時程，增加股債市場波動。

歐元區通膨率升至近三年新高，９月升息機率高
• 歐洲央行9月升息機率高：歐洲央行（ECB）9月升息至2.50%的預期已逐步升溫，主因歐元區8月通膨升至3.3%，加上能源
價格受伊朗戰事推升，強化鷹派對進一步緊縮的主張。不過，核心通膨降至2.4%、服務通膨趨緩，且長期通膨預期仍大致穩
定，顯示能源價格尚未明顯向薪資與核心物價擴散，因此，9月升息後暫停仍是較可能的政策路徑。 9月升息後，ECB是否於
12月再次升息，將取決於能源衝擊的持續時間及其向核心通膨的傳導程度。
• 12月是否再升息，能源價格是關鍵：若伊朗戰事延續、油氣價格維持高檔，鷹派可能推動利率於12月進一步升至2.75%；反
之，若能源價格回落、服務通膨持續降溫，則本輪升息可能在9月告終。整體而言，ECB政策已轉向偏鷹，但在缺乏能源衝

擊廣泛傳導至核心通膨的明確證據下，年內連續升息空間有限。在政策利率逐步觸頂的情況下，歐債殖利率若因升息預期而
上行，反而可提供較佳的中長期配置與收益機會。
歐元區通膨（CPI） ECBspeak指數暗示歐洲央行還會進一步加息
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.2 資料來源：，2026.902

韓企業績展望在第⼆季財報加速改善
• 韓企業績展望在第⼆季財報加速改善 ：儘管市場波動劇烈 ，韓國企業在第⼆季財報的 獲利前景仍在持續改善 ，在三星電⼦和
SK海力⼠公佈業績後，MSCI韓國指數的全年每股純益預期被小幅上調 ，此前5至6月修正步伐一度放緩，如今⼜重新加快，整
體來看今年韓國獲利預期累計上調幅度接近 190%，並推動整個新興市場 獲利預期提升約37%，晶⽚需求指引依然強勁 ，隨著
獲利預測持續上調，台灣市場也從中受益。
• 展望後市：在韓國政府推動Value-up政策下，企業愈來愈重視股東權益 ，透過提高現金股利及擴大 買回庫藏股回饋股東，有
望成為第三季韓股上漲的重要動能，尤其是記憶體產業龍頭企業，擁有充沛的自由現金流，實施庫藏股買回，不僅能提升每股
價值，也反映管理層對未來獲利前景具信心，並認為目前股價被低估，參考日本推動公司治理改革後帶動股市重評價的經驗 ，

預期韓股後市仍具支撐力，若市場出現短期回檔，反而可視為逢低布局的機會。

期戰略方向，相關受惠企業可望持續成長 ，另一方面科網龍頭的AI業務已逐步走出高投入階段，開始展現商業化成果與獲利

美債市場結構性轉變：從不挑剔的機構到追求回報的私人投資者
• 自新冠疫情以來 ，美國10年期公債殖利率持續走高 ，過去市場多半歸咎於聯準會升息或通膨 ，但彭博最新研究顯示 ，期限
溢價的顯著上升才是主要推手，其背後的根本原因是美債買家發生了結構性轉變。
• 傳統非價格敏感型買家縮手：過去幾十年，聯準會(透過QE)與外國央行是美債的兩大底氣來源，他們買美債是為了儲備與流
動性，對價格不敏感，因此能有效壓低利率與期限溢價 。自2020年以來，聯準會與外國官方持有的美債佔美國 GDP比重分
別下降12%與8%，光是這兩大買家的退場，就推升10年期美債期限溢價約1.05%。
• 私人投資者接棒，市場對殖利率極度挑剔： 目前的邊際買家已轉為外國私人投資者 (如基金、保險公司等)，私人買家會精算

美債、公司債、股票與各國主權債的收益差異，進行嚴格比價。當美債發行量增加時，必須給予更高的殖利率才能吸引他們
購買，這使得美債利率波動更加劇烈。
自疫情以來，長期利率呈上升趨勢
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.1
主要機構買家持有的美國國債減少

供給端新挑戰：AI浪潮帶動公司債暴增，搶奪資金池
• 科技巨頭發債融資潮 ，公司債成為美債的強勁替代品： 受人工智慧建設資本支出帶動 ，大型科技企業 (如Alphabet、Meta、
Amazon等)積極發行投資級公司債 。這些高品質科技公司債提供優異回報 ，吸引了大量私人資金從美債轉向公司債 。為了
與這些高收益的高評級公司債競爭資金，美國國債殖利率面臨更大的上行壓力。
• 小結及建議：當前美債殖利率居高不下，核心原因在於穩定的買家減少，而接手的私人買家變得挑剔，加上AI熱潮創造了高
回報的公司債競爭源。在美國財政部發債壓力未減的情況下，長端殖利率短期內難大幅下行。債券策略建議採取配置中短天
期債券，長天期公債(10年期以上)價格受期限溢價與供給壓力壓制，波動較大，建議縮短持債年期(如布局3-5年期)，鎖定高

殖利率的同時降低價格波動風險 ，加碼高品質投資級公司債，目前私人資金偏好高評級公司債(尤其是科技與AI龍頭債)，這
類債券信用風險極低，收益率卻優於同天期公債，是當前固定收益資產的優質選擇。
外國私人投資者購買美國公債和公司債
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.1
人工智慧相關債務發行只是殖利率上升的原因之一

日圓再現匯率干預跡象 套利交易平倉恐加劇日圓升值
• 日圓再現匯率干預跡象：上週週間，日圓兌美元匯價出現單日大幅升值情況，市場再度懷疑官方出手干預匯率。然而，彭博
指出，日本央行帳戶數據沒有大規模買入日元跡象，意味著匯率的劇烈波動可能來自市場重新調整日本央行加息預期，而非
日本政府再次入市干預。與此同時，美國財政部並未回應是否參與干預。
• 市場懷疑干預之際，套利交易平倉可能正在發生：儘管無法證實官方上週是否直接干預日圓匯價，但日圓走升可能也反映上
週日本央行官員高田創釋出鷹派升息的態度，將不排除一次性大幅升息或連續升息的可能性。根據芝加哥商品交易所的數據
顯示，日圓融資的套利交易正在平倉，而且中期內持有日圓而非其他G10貨幣的意願非常強烈。
• 觀點：2024年8月日本央行意外升息，引發日圓套利交易平倉，全球主要國家股市短期間出現修正，當時AI與半導體成為套

利資金撤離重點。本次匯價波動暫時可歸咎於套利交易倉位的調整，尚未出現恐慌賣壓，重點將關注在本月中的日本央行利
率會議，留意意外大幅升息的風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.9.3。

• 今年年初黃金價格創下歷史新高，突破每盎司5,000美元大關，成為金融市場最受關注的資產之一，隨後經歷幾個月的高檔
修正後，上個月再度獲得市場資金追捧，現貨黃金價格單月大漲近10%。過去市場認為黃金上漲主要來自地緣政治風險與央
行降息預期，但近期投資邏輯似乎正在改變。
• 黃金多頭始自央行「去美元化」：本輪黃金多頭始自2022年烏俄衝突，西方國家凍結俄羅斯央行的大部分海外美元與歐元
外匯儲備，讓各國央行開始意識到外匯儲備的安全性，進而推動外匯儲備多元化。透過適度減持美元(美債)來佈局實體黃金
或其他國家貨幣，被成為「去美元化」趨勢。
• 「貶值交易」有望接棒黃金上漲動能：上個月美國公布最新國債規模已突破40兆美元，市場開始擔憂財政赤字的可維持性，
貨幣信心下降導致「貶值交易」發生(美債信心下降)，除了導致長天期美債殖利率上揚之外，貨幣避險需求再度讓黃金資產

成為市場焦點，8月份現貨黃金一度上漲超過15%，隨後雖因聯準會釋出鷹派訊號，單月漲幅仍接近10%。
貶值交易趨勢下，黃金的投資機會
全球央行黃金儲備佔比

• 央行買盤仍為黃金中期表現帶來利多：根據世界黃金協會2026年各國央行調查，上半年截至5/19，有45%的央行預期未來

及金價長期上漲趨勢，顯示黃金已成為各國儲備資產的重要戰略配置，在強勁的需求買盤下，多數機構預測黃金將維持多頭
至明年年底。
• 觀點：近期黃金價格反彈後，可能會受到獲利了結以及利率政策預期變化而出現波動，但在全球債務問題、貨幣貶值風險及
央行買盤支撐下，在中期內仍有機會走出慢牛行情。建議投資人以資產配置的角度，逢修正適度配置黃金資產(如：黃金ETF)。
風險接受度較高的投資人，則可關注金礦類股，但需注意其對於美元匯、利率的敏感度較高，資產價格波動較大。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。
警 語

Document 045: 2026年9月FOMCプレビュー ～利上げと据え置きそれぞれのメリット・デメリット～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月FOMCプレビュー ～利上げと据え置きそれぞれのメリット・デメリット～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-09-08
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

US Trends

2026 年 9 月 8 日

1/6 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC プレビュー 
～利上げと据え置きそれぞれのメリット・デメリット～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  前田 和馬 
（℡：050-5473-3888）

（要旨） 
○ 9 月 FOMC（9/15～16 開催）に関して、9 月 7 日時点の FF 金利先物市場では約 6 割の確率で
0.25%pt の利上げ、4 割の確率で金利据え置きが織り込まれている。9 月 FOMC における政策
判断は、9/11 公表の8 月 CPI でインフレの減速傾向が保たれるかに大きく依存する。 
○ ウォーシュ議長は8 月28 日のジャクソンホール講演において、 「インフレが明確かつ十分な
速度で2%へ向かわなければ、 やるべき仕事がある」 と述べるなど、9 月FOMC における利上げ
の可能性を示唆した。一方、ウォラー理事やニューヨーク連銀・ウィリアムズ総裁は利上げ
に慎重な姿勢を示すなど、現時点において主要メンバー間の見解は統一されていない。 
○ 8 月 CPI が市場予想通りに着地し FRB が利上げを決定する場合、ウォーシュ議長は「インフ
レ抑制に向けた強い姿勢」や「金融政策の政治からの独立性」を示すことができよう。しか
し、11 月に中間選挙を控えるなか、トランプ大統領は利上げ判断を非難する可能性が高い。 
○ 一方、ウォーシュ発言後に利上げ観測が高まったにもかかわらず政策金利を据え置けば、金
融市場では FRB のコミュニケーション不信を巡る懸念が強まるだろう。とはいえ、こうした
言動不一致は FRB 高官発言の重要性を低下させ、ウォーシュ議長の望む「金融市場が自ら景
気動向を評価する（経済指標を注視する）」状態を形成するのに役立つ可能性もある。 
○ 今後の政策金利パスを見極めるうえでは、同時に公表されるドットチャートが注目される。
9 月に利上げに踏み切る場合には今後の利上げペース、金利が据え置かれる場合には 2026～
27 年における利上げの可能性が意識されるだろう。なお、ウォーシュ議長は 6 月 FOMC と同
様に自身の予測を提出しない可能性が高い。

### Page 2

2/6 
・予想が割れる 9 月FOMC 
9 月FOMC（9/15～16 開催）における政策金利判断は、金融市場の予想が大きく割れている。過
去 2 か月における9 月FOMCの政策予想をみると（資料 1）、7 月分の雇用統計（8/7 公表）や消費者
物価指数（8/12）が軟調だったことで据え置き予想が優勢だった一方、ウォーシュ議長のジャク
ソンホール会議講演（8/28）が予想に反してタカ派だったことで0.25%の利上げ予想が強まった。
その後、ウォラー理事など他のFRB高官がタカ派の見方に追随しなかったため、利上げ織り込みが
幾分剥落したものの、8 月雇用統計（9/4）が市場予想を上回ったことで再び利上げ予想が優勢と
なった。この結果、本稿執筆時点におけるFF金利先物市場（Fed Watch）において、約 6 割の確率
で 0.25%ptの利上げ、4 割の確率で金利据え置きが織り込まれている。なお、利上げとなれば2023
年 7 月以来、据え置きとなれば6 会合連続となる。

資料 1： 9 月 FOMC における利上げ織り込み 
  資料 2：市場が予想する各FOMC における政策金利
の確率分布（9/7 時点）

出所: CME、FRB、EIA より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成

8 月地区連銀経済報告（9 月2 日公表：8 月 24 日までの情報に基づく）では前回報告の評価が概
ね据え置かれた。すなわち「12 地区中10 地区で経済活動は小幅から緩やかに成長した」とまとめ
られ、消費では高級品や観光、製造業では防衛やデータセンター関連の堅調さがそれぞれ指摘さ
れた。一方、インフレのペースは「前回報告から8 地区で横ばい、3 地区で減少、1 地区で増加」
と述べられた。また、雇用は「ごく僅かに増加」と前回表現の「総じて増加」から下方修正され
た。ただ、これは8 月雇用統計公表前の評価であることに留意が必要だ。8 月の非農業部門雇用者
数は前月差+16.2 万人と市場予想（+5.5 万人）を大幅に上回ったほか、過去2 か月分も上方修正
された。この結果、3 か月移動平均では+7.1 万人となるなど、高齢化やデータセンター需要を背
景に、医療・介護や卸・小売、建設業などで回復の動きが続いている。

### Page 3

3/6 
9 月FOMCまでには9/10 に 8 月生産者物価指数（PPI）、9/11 に8 月消費者物価指数（CPI）、9/16
に 8 月小売売上高が公表される。最大の注目は8 月CPIであり、市場予想は総合指数が前月比
+0.4%（7 月実績：+0.1%）とガソリン価格を中心に加速する一方、食料とエネルギーを除くコア指
数は+0.2%（同+0.2%）と横ばい圏の推移が見込まれている。コアCPIが市場予想を上回り8 月コア
PCE（9/30 公表）においてもインフレ加速が見込まれる場合、9 月FOMCにおける利上げ織り込みが
進むだろう。一方、過去2 か月と同様にインフレの減速傾向が確認されれば、据え置き予想がや
や強まる可能性がある。

・ウォーシュ議長のタカ派シフト 
7 月FOMCは 5 会合連続で政策金利が据え置かれ、声明文における景気・物価判断は前回から変更
がなかった。すなわち、経済活動は「中東紛争による不確実性の高まりにも関わらず、経済活動は
堅調なペースで拡大」 、 労働市場は 「雇用増は労働力人口の拡大と歩調を合わせており、 失業率はほ
ぼ変化していない」 、物価は「インフレ率は+2%目標と比べて高止まりしている。この背景には供給
ショックがエネルギーを含む一部のセクターの物価を押し上げていることがある」 と、 それぞれま
とめられた。本稿執筆時点において、こうした評価を大幅に変えうる材料は特段見当たらない。 
一方、ウォーシュ議長はジャクソンホール講演（8/28）において、8 月分の物価指標の結果が良
好でなければ9 月FOMCで利上げに踏み切る可能性を示唆した。 具体的には、自身の発言がフォワー
ドガイダンスではないと前置きしたうえで、 「今夏のPCEとCPIは予想よりも良好だった一方、基調
的なインフレが大きく改善したとは思えない」や「基調的なインフレが目標に向かって明確かつ十
分なスピードで進展しないならば、やるべきことがある」と述べた。ウォーシュ議長が利上げを提
案する場合、7 月FOMCでも利上げを主張した3 名の地区連銀総裁のほか（クリーブランド連銀のハ
マック総裁、 ミネアポリス連銀のカシュカリ総裁、 ダラス連銀のローガン総裁） 、 バー理事やクック
理事などの多くの執行部メンバーがこれに同調する可能性が高い。 
一方、利上げに慎重な向きも残る。9/2 にニューヨーク連銀のウィリアムズ総裁はインフレの緩
やかな鈍化傾向がみられるとし、現状の政策金利が適切な水準にあると指摘した。また、9/3 には
ウォラー理事が、一部品目の推計手法変更がコアPCEインフレ率の下方修正に繋がる可能性を指摘
したうえで、「インフレ率が目標に向けて進展していることを確認できれば、政策金利の据え置き
を支持する」と主張した。仮に 9 月FOMCにて利上げが決定される場合においても、ウォラー理事や
ボウマン理事は据え置きを主張し反対票を投じる可能性がある。

・それぞれの判断に対するメリデメ 
9 月FOMCの政策判断は今後公表される8 月分の物価指標に大きく依存する。一方、仮に物価動向
が概ね市場予想と一致する場合、9 月の政策判断は次のような意味合いを持つだろう。 
まず、利上げへと踏み切る場合、FRBは物価安定を重視する姿勢を改めて示すことができる。特に
足下でインフレ減速の兆しがみられるなか、基調的なインフレトレンドの低下が十分でないとして
利上げに踏み切ることは、FRBがインフレ抑制を重視する姿勢を鮮明にする。 また、 ウォーシュ議長

### Page 4

4/6 
は利下げを求めるトランプ政権への配慮から利上げに否定的との見方 が就任以前からあるものの、
9 月FOMCでの利上げはこうした見方を払しょくし、金融政策判断の政治的独立性を示すだろう。も
っとも、11 月の中間選挙を控えるなかでの利上げはトランプ政権の批判を招く可能性が高い。実
際、9/4 にトランプ大統領は 「米国の金利はどの国よりも低くあるべき」 、 バンス副大統領は 「イン
フレ指標は利下げを正当化する内容」とそれぞれ主張している。 
一方、政策金利を据え置く場合、 ウォーシュ議長のジャクソンホール講演を受けて利上げ観測が
高まっただけに、金融市場ではFRBのコミュニケーション手法に対する不信感を強めるだろう。ウ
ォーシュ議長は「インフレの明確かつ十分な進展」の基準を明言したわけではないが、一般論とし
て考えると、8 月分の物価指標がインフレ鈍化を明確に示す内容でなければ、据え置き判断との整
合性は取りにくい。また、金利据え置きは「ウォーシュ議長がトランプ政権に配慮し利上げに踏み
切れない」との見方を強めることになる。とはいえ、 据え置きの場合には金融市場がFRB高官発言へ
の注目度を低下させるとみられ、これはウォーシュ議長が望む「金融市場がFRB高官の発言を過度
に気にするのではなく、自ら景気・物価動向を判断する」状態に近づける効果を持つ。

・ドットチャートの示す政策パス 
 9 月FOMC後には四半期経済見通し （SEP）が公表される。 前回6 月時点の政策金利見通し （ドット
チャートの中央値）は、2026 年が 3.75%（0.5 回の利上げ） 、27 年は 3.625%（0.5 回の利下げ；現
在の金利水準）、28 年は3.375%（1 回の利下げ）となり、前回 3 月FOMC（2026～27 年にそれぞれ1
回ずつの利下げを予想）から上方修正された。仮に9 月FOMCで利上げへと踏み切る場合、今後の焦
点は追加的な利上げの有無やその回数に移行する。例えば、7 月FOMCで利上げを主張したダラス連
銀のローガン総裁は「短期的に控えめな利上げ」に言及する一方、ミネアポリス連銀のカシュカリ
総裁は複数回の利上げの可能性を指摘している。 このため、新たなドットチャートでは、26～27 年
における追加の利上げ回数、28 年に利下げサイクルへ戻るのか否か、さらに旺盛なAI投資を背景に
中立金利（longer run）の上昇が示唆されるのか、などが注目される。一方、9 月FOMCが据え置き
判断となる場合、26～27 年において利上げを予想するタカ派と据え置きを見込むハト派の分布が
焦点となるだろう。

・FRB高官発言

7/31

ダラス連銀 
ローガン総裁* 
7月FOMCで利上げを主張した理由として、インフレ率が+2%に回帰す
る軌道にはなく、金融引締めなしではインフレ高止まりが続くと主
張し、短期的に控えめな利上げを求めた 。また、現在の緩やかな引
き締めが、将来の急激な引き締めの可能性を低下させると述べた

ミネアポリス連銀 
カシュカリ総裁* 
7月FOMCで利上げを主張した背景として、 「供給ショックは見過ごす
べきとの考えに概ね賛同する一方、インフレ長期化に繋がりうる一
連の供給ショックに対して、金融政策は重要な役割を果たす」と主
張した。また、インフレが高止まりする場合、複数回の利上げを実
施すべきとの見解を示した 
8/27

ハマック総裁* 
インフレの高止まりが続いており、2027年もインフレ率が良くても
2%台半ばまでしか低下しないとの自身の予測に基づくと、現時点で
利上げを実施すべきとの考えを示した

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

ボストン連銀 
コリンズ総裁 
足下のインフレ指標は利上げを促すものではないと指摘したほか、
ベースシナリオとしてインフレの緩やかな低下を予想すると述べ
た。一方、インフレに進展が見られない場合は利上げが適切との考
えを示した

カンザスシティ連銀 
シュミッド総裁 
「インフレは頑固で粘着的」とし、現行の政策金利は抑制的ではな
いと指摘した。 一方、9月で利上げを支持するかを巡っては 「もう少
し情報が必要」と述べるなど、データを注視する姿勢を示した 
8/28 ウォーシュ議長* 
「今夏のPCEとCPIは予想よりも良好だった一方、基調的なインフレ
が大きく改善したとは思えない」 や「総合的にみて、広範な金融環
境を制約的とみるのは難しい」 と述べるなど、現行の政策金利がイ
ンフレ抑制に適切ではない可能性を示唆した。また、コミュニケー
ション手法を巡っては「平時において、フォワードガイダンスの役
割は限定的かつ制限されるべき」と従来の主張を繰り返した 
9/1 バー理事* 
「インフレは5年以上にわたり高止まりしている」と述べ、9月FOMC
において「インフレ率が十分に鈍化しているようにみえない場合、
断固として利上げを行うべき」と明言した 
9/2 
ニューヨーク連銀 
ウィリアムズ総裁* 
インフレは緩やかな低下傾向にあり、現行の政策金利は「適切な水
準」と述べるなど、9月FOMCで様子見姿勢をとる可能性を示唆した。
また、足下の長期金利上昇はAI投資需要などの強い経済を反映して
いるとの見方を示した 
9/3 ウォラー理事* 
9月FOMCにおける政策判断は 8月のインフレ動向に強く依存すると
し、「2%目標への進展が継続するならば、現状維持を支持する」と
述べた。 また、FRBの政策反応関数をどのようにコミュニケーション
するかを巡って、野球の球審のように完璧なストライクゾーンでは
なく「大まかな目安」を示す必要性を主張した

Document 161: 70%项目注定被砍？Claude Code成功背后，Anthropic养了一支“失败者”团队

Source PDF: all report pdf/70%项目注定被砍？Claude Code成功背后，Anthropic养了一支“失败者”团队.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-08
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 内部不⾜ 20 ⼈的 Labs 团队，以 “ ⾼淘汰率换创新速度 ” 著称。项⽬每两周接受⼀次
“ 存活或转向 ” 评审，约 70% ⾄ 80% 的 “ 押注 ” 最终被砍，少数成功项⽬独⽴孵化， Claude
Code 便是其中之⼀。依托贴近模型研发前沿的优势， Labs 持续将新能⼒快速产品化，并通
过真实应⽤反哺模型迭代。
摘要：
⼀个成功的 Claude Code 背后，可能站着更多已经被砍掉的项⽬。
据 Business Insider 报道， Anthropic 内部有⼀⽀不⾜ 20 ⼈的 “Labs” 团队，专⻔负责做这件事：不
断提出新产品、快速验证，然后砍掉⼤多数失败的尝试。据团队负责⼈、 Anthropic 联合创始⼈
Ben Mann 透露，Labs 项⽬的成功率仅约 20% ⾄ 30% ，换⾔之，⼤约 70% ⾄ 80% 的 “ 押注 ” 最终
都会失败。
但 Anthropic 并不把失败视为浪费。恰恰相反，这套⾼淘汰率机制正在成为公司把前沿模型能⼒

快速变成商业产品的重要引擎。 Claude Code 、模型上下⽂协议（ MCP ）以及 Claude Design 等
产品，均与 Labs 有关。随着 Anthropic 筹备 IPO ，这⽀ “ 失败者 ” 团队的重要性也正在上升。
Mann 在接受采访时表⽰：“ 我们确实将其视为真正意义上的创业孵化器，并预期⼤多数押注会
以失败告终。 ”在 Anthropic 看来，关键不是让每个项⽬都成功，⽽是⽤⾜够低的试错成本，换取
少数⾜以改变业务曲线的 “ 命中 ” 。
两周⼀轮 “ 存活还是转向 ” ：⼤多数项⽬注定被砍
Labs 的运作⽅式与传统产品团队截然不同。约 20 名成员采⽤轮换制，持续提出新的产品原型，
内部称之为 “ 押注 ” （ bets ）。每隔约两周，团队就会进⾏⼀次 “ 坚持还是转向 ” （ persevere or pivot ）
评审：表现不佳的项⽬被直接砍掉或并⼊其他项⽬，成员随即转向新的尝试；少数被验证的项
⽬则从 Labs“ 毕业 ” ，独⽴组建团队。
Claude Code 就是其中最成功的⼀次 “ 押注 ” 。 Mann 最初将任务交给刚⼊职不久的 Boris Cherny 。

Cherny ⼀开始想做的是代码分析⼯具，但 Mann 认为这⼀⽅向不够⼤胆，要求他把⽬标做得更
⼤。随后， Cherny 开发出的原型迅速在 Anthropic 内部⾛红，并于 2025 年 2 ⽉以预览版形式对外
发布。
此后，随着 Claude 模型持续升级， Claude Code 逐渐从⼀个内部原型成⻓为 Anthropic 最具影
响⼒的产品之⼀，也成为公司增⻓提速的重要催化剂。
Labs 能够⾼频试错，还有⼀个关键优势：它离研究前沿⾜够近。 Mann 表⽰， Claude Code 开发
启动前，团队便从研究⼈员处了解到新模型在智能体编程⽅⾯展现出的潜⼒，因此能够在模型
能⼒成熟之前提前布局，将研究成果更快推向产品端。
产品版图持续扩张：从编程⼯具到设计软件
Claude Code 并⾮ Labs 唯⼀的成果。团队还孵化了模型上下⽂协议（ Model Context Protocol ，
MCP ），⽤于连接 AI 智能体与外部数据源；今年推出的 Claude Design 同样来⾃ Labs 。

Instagram 联合创始⼈ Mike Krieger 也在这⽀团队中。

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
Mann 将 Labs 的⽬标定义为扩⼤ AI 的 “ ⾏动空间 ” （ action space ），也就是不断拓展 AI 模型能够真
正完成的任务边界。因此， Labs 做的不只是寻找下⼀个爆款产品，也会在产品实践中暴露模型
能⼒的短板，再推动研究团队补⻬。
例如， Labs 曾推动 Anthropic 研究团队改善⾳频模型对埃塞俄⽐亚阿姆哈拉语的理解能⼒；
Claude Design 的开发，则进⼀步激发了公司对视觉输出能⼒的研究兴趣。产品试错因此不再只
是验证商业需求，也成为反向推动模型能⼒进化的⼀条路径。
这种机制如今也开始向 Labs 之外扩散。 Mann 透露， Anthropic 其他产品⼯程团队⽬前也设⽴了
类似的 “ 押注 ” ⼩组。
IPO 前后的⾼难度平衡：既要敢于失败，也要直⾯竞争

随着 Anthropic 筹备 IPO ， Labs 需要解决的问题也不再只是 “ 能不能做出下⼀个 Claude Code” ，⽽
是如何在持续创新与商业化之间找到平衡。
Forrester 分析师 Mike Gualtieri 指出， Anthropic 正在⾛⼀条 “ ⾼空钢丝 ” ：公司不断推出的新⼯
具，可能直接进⼊企业客⼾现有软件的市场。以 Claude Design 为例，其产品能⼒已经开始与
Adobe 、 Figma 等设计软件形成竞争。
Gualtieri 认为，随着 Anthropic 逐步构建⾃⼰的产品平台，公司可能创造出⾜以让⽤⼾减少对其
他软件依赖的功能。 “ 这将是⼀场微妙的博弈。 ”
与此同时， Labs 本⾝也必须保持⾜够⾼的⼈员流动。项⽬通常在团队规模超过四⼈后便会 “ 毕业 ”
并独⽴出去，成员不断流向其他部⻔，同时⼜持续有新⼈加⼊。这意味着，Labs 的核⼼不是⼀
⽀固定的 20 ⼈团队，⽽是⼀套不断制造、筛选和放⼤产品机会的机制。
更⻓远来看， Mann 希望这种机制能够从软件产品延伸⾄⽣物研究、清洁能源存储等硬科学领

域，让 AI 真正进⼊更⼴泛的现实世界。他认为，加速硬科学发展的能⼒ “ 令⼈极度兴奋 ” ，也是 AI
开始创造更⼤现实价值的关键。

### Page 1

申万宏观认为， 8 ⽉出⼝同⽐升⾄ 25% ，⾼于预期，三条主线共同⽀撑：⼀是 AI 出⼝⾼增
⻓，涨价推升名义读数；⼆是消费品出⼝延续改善，受美国进⼝回补与 “ 供给替代 ” 共同影
响；三是⾮ AI ⽣产资料及对欧盟、东盟出⼝⾛弱。进⼝延续⾼增，主因 AI 商品涨价，⼤宗
商品转弱。后续出⼝或维持韧性， AI 与消费领域或是主要⽀撑。
摘要：
摘要
事件： 9 ⽉ 8 ⽇，海关总署公布 8 ⽉进出⼝数据，出⼝（以美元计价）同⽐ 25.0% 、预期 21.9% 、
前值 23.9% ；进⼝（以美元计价）同⽐ 28.2% 、预期 28.1% 、前值 27.6% 。
核⼼观点：出⼝⾼增源于 AI 出⼝涨价与消费品出⼝改善，但⾮ AI ⽣产资料有所⾛弱。
8 ⽉台⻛天⽓扰动下，出⼝仍维持较⾼增速。8 ⽉，出⼝同⽐（美元计价）较 7 ⽉回升 1.1 个百分
点⾄ 25.0% ；环⽐ (0.9%) 符合季节性（ 0.8% ）。受台⻛等极端天⽓增多影响， 8 ⽉港⼝货物吞吐
量单⽉同⽐较 7 ⽉下⾏ 7.4 个百分点；但出⼝链实际⽣产增速（剔除价格）基本稳定于 -0.8% ，领

先出⼝ 1 个⽉的加⼯贸易进⼝也维持⾼增速 (7 ⽉同⽐ 36.5% ）。
主线⼀：造成⾼频与出⼝背离的原因仍在于 AI 出⼝⾼增⻓，尤其是涨价影响了名义读数。分商
品看，集成电路、⾃动数据处理设备及零部件等 AI 商品出⼝是⽀撑总出⼝改善的主要⼒量，两者
同⽐分别回升 13.3 、 9.1 个百分点⾄ 129.8% 、 76.5% ；其中价格或为主要贡献，测算集成电路商
品价格同⽐较 7 ⽉回升 36.8 个百分点⾄ 149.6% 。国别层⾯， AI ⾼景⽓也推动对美国、韩国出⼝⾛
强，同⽐分别回升 17.4 、 2.6 个百分点⾄ 34.6% 、 49.5% 。
主线⼆：消费品出⼝延续改善也是⽀撑出⼝⾼增的重要线索，背后或与美国进⼝回升周期、 “ 供
给替代 ” 效应等共同影响。8 ⽉玩具（回升 17.0 个百分点⾄ 15.3% ）、鞋靴（回升 8.7 个百分点⾄
11.4% ）、家⽤电器（回升 4.1 个百分点⾄ 10.8%) 等商品出⼝均明显回升；或反映消费品出⼝ “ 供
给替代 ” 效应与美国进⼝回升周期的共同影响。 8 ⽉对美出⼝回升 17.4 个百分点，或显⽰前期关税

导致美国进⼝周期后置，继续⽀撑我国出⼝。
主线三：⾮ AI ⽣产资料、对欧盟东盟出⼝有所⾛弱，或与⾼油价影响相关地区⽣产有关。8 ⽉船
舶（回落 71.3 个百分点）、通⽤机械（回落 16.2 个百分点）、未锻轧铝及铝材（回落 6.3 个百分
点）、液晶平板模组、塑料制品等商品出⼝明显回落；上述出⼝与欧盟东盟⽣产⾛势⾼度⼀致。
持续⾼油价背景下， 8 ⽉东盟⽣产增速边际下⾏，欧盟⽣产增速持续弱于去年底。国别层⾯，对
欧盟、东盟出⼝分别回落 9.4 、 8.2 个百分点⾄ 6.8% 、 30.4% 。
8 ⽉进⼝延续⾼增，或继续体现 AI 商品涨价对进⼝的影响，⼤宗商品进⼝则有所转弱。8 ⽉进⼝
（美元计价）同⽐较 7 ⽉回升 0.6 个百分点⾄ 28.2% ，⾃动数据处理设备及其零部件、集成电路
（ 71.1% ）等 AI 相关商品进⼝分别回升 15.3 、 12.5 个百分点⾄ 209.1% 、 83.6% ，也与 AI 商品涨
价有关。⼤宗商品表现有所⾛弱，原油、铁矿⽯、铜、⼤⾖进⼝分别回落 4.3 、 7.5 、 2.0 、 2.6 个

百分点⾄ -8.9% 、 6.2% 、 24.7% 、 7.3% 。

### Page 2

重申观点： 9 ⽉后出⼝整体或维持较强韧性， AI 与消费领域或是主要⽀撑。全球 AI ⾰命或继续⽀
撑出⼝， AI 相关出⼝或维持较⾼增速，尤其是价格⽅⾯的⽀撑。同时，消费出⼝或维持较强韧
性，背后除了消费品 “ 供给替代 ” 效应显现外，关税导致美国进⼝周期后置，或也对我国消费品出
⼝形成⽀撑；按库存周期规律看，这⼀逻辑或持续演绎半年以上。
常规跟踪： 8 ⽉出⼝、进⼝同⽐回升。
消 费 品 ⽅ ⾯ ， 消 费 电 ⼦ 、 轻 ⼯ 制 品 出 ⼝ 增 速 回 升 。 消 费 电 ⼦ 出 ⼝ 增 速 回 升 8 . 6 个 百 分 点 ⾄
79.7% ，其中⾃动数据处理设备及其零部件、⼿机出⼝均有所回升；轻⼯制品出⼝增速回升 2.5
个百分点⾄ 9.3% ，箱包、服装、鞋靴出⼝改善较为明显。
资本品出⼝增速回落，中间品表现分化，能源资源品出⼝增速回升。资本品⽅⾯，船舶、通⽤
机械出⼝增速均明显回落；中间品⽅⾯，汽⻋零配件出⼝增速回落，集成电路出⼝增速回升；
能源资源品⽅⾯，成品油出⼝⼤幅回升，稀⼟、未锻轧铝及铝材出⼝回落。

国别⽅⾯，对美国出⼝增速⼤幅回升。发达经济体中，对美国出⼝增速回升，对欧盟出⼝增速
回落；新兴经济体和地区中，对东盟出⼝增速回落，对俄罗斯、拉美出⼝回升。

23.9% ；进⼝（以美元计价）同⽐ 28.2% 、预期 28.1% 、前值 27.6% 。

点⾄ 25.0% ；环⽐（ 0.9% ）符合季节性（ 0.8% ）。受台⻛等极端天⽓增多影响， 8 ⽉港⼝货物吞

### Page 5

主线⼆：消费品出⼝延续改善也是⽀撑出⼝⾼增的重要线索，背后或与美国进⼝回升周期、 “ 供
给替代 ” 效应等共同影响。8 ⽉消费品出⼝延续改善，玩具（回升 17.0 个百分点⾄ 15.3% ）、鞋靴
（回升 8.7 个百分点⾄ 11.4% ）、家⽤电器（回升 4.1 个百分点⾄ 10.8%) 等商品出⼝增速均明显回
升；从驱动因素来看，前期关税导致美国消费品进⼝相对消费 “ 低估 ” ，令消费品库存处于极低⽔
平。在此背景下，虽然美国消费并未⾛强，但低库存带动消费品进⼝明显回补，⽀撑我国消费
品出⼝，也即关税导致美国进⼝周期后置于今年的影响， 8 ⽉对美出⼝回升 17.4 个百分点。此
外，中国⽣产表现持续优于海外的 “ 供给替代 ” 效应也构成适度⽀撑。
主线三：⾮ AI ⽣产资料、对欧盟东盟出⼝有所⾛弱，或与⾼油价影响相关地区⽣产有关。8 ⽉⾮
AI ⽣产资料出⼝有所⾛弱，譬如船舶（回落 71.3 个百分点⾄ 21.0% ）、通⽤机械设备（回落 16.2
个百分点⾄ 15.2% ）、未锻轧铝及铝材（回落 6.3 个百分点⾄ 38.5% ）、液晶平板模组（回落 2.1 个

百分点⾄ -2.9% ）、塑料制品（回落 1.2 个百分点⾄ 15.2% ）等商品出⼝明显回落；从驱动因素
看，⾮ AI ⽣产资料与东盟和欧盟⽣产⾛势⾼度⼀致。持续⾼油价背景下，东盟⽣产 8 ⽉以来出现
边际放缓迹象（ 8 ⽉越南⽣产指数同⽐边际下⾏ 1.1 个百分点⾄ 19.0% ），欧盟⽣产增速（ 1.0% ）
也持续低于去年年底表现（ 1.9% ）。国别层⾯， 8 ⽉对欧盟、东盟出⼝分别回落 9.4 、 8.2 个百分
点⾄ 6.8% 、 30.4% 。

### Page 8

展望后续： 9 ⽉后出⼝整体或维持较强韧性， AI 与消费领域仍将构成主要⽀撑。全球 AI ⾰命有望
继续⽀撑出⼝， AI 相关商品出⼝或维持较⾼增速，尤其是价格⽅⾯的⽀撑。同时，消费出⼝或维
持较强韧性，背后除了消费品 “ 供给替代 ” 效应显现外，关税导致美国进⼝周期后置，或也对我国
消费品出⼝形成⽀撑；按库存周期与进⼝周期的规律看，这⼀逻辑或持续演绎半年以上。

增速回升（ +8.6pct ⾄ 79.7% ），结构上⾃动数据处理设备及其零部件（ +9.1pct ⾄ 76.5% ）回升、

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Commodities: From Inflation Hedge to Structural Growth Asset
Anna Snider, Managing Director and Head of Investment Manager Selection
(Due Diligence)
A broader role in portfolios: For decades, commodities occupied a relatively narrow
role in portfolios: protection against inflation when rising prices eroded the value
of traditional assets. That function remains important, but the investment case is

broadening. Commodities now sit at the intersection of geopolitical fragmentation,
supply-chain security, reshoring, inventory rebuilding, and the buildout of AI and digital
infrastructure. Together, these forces are turning parts of the asset class from purely
cyclical exposures into potential long-term growth beneficiaries.
The diversification case is also becoming more relevant. In Q1, when inflation concerns
peaked, the Bloomberg Commodity Index returned close to 25%, outperforming both

Equities and Fixed Income. At the same time, the relationship among major asset
classes has shifted. Equity and Fixed Income correlations have tightened in recent years,
while commodities have moved in the opposite direction: over the trailing five years,
their correlation with both Equities and bonds has been negative. That combination of
inflation sensitivity and differentiated return behavior can make commodities useful
beyond a simple tactical hedge.

AI infrastructure is a physical buildout: AI is often viewed through semiconductors,
software platforms, and hyperscale data centers. Yet the infrastructure required to
deploy AI is extraordinarily commodity intensive. It requires continuous electricity,
generation capacity, transmission lines, substations, transformers, cooling systems,
networking hardware and structural materials. In that sense, AI is not only a computing
revolution. It is also an energy, infrastructure, and critical-materials investment cycle.

Power is the first constraint. Data centers require reliable, around-the-clock electricity.
Natural gas remains a practical source of incremental generation in the U.S., while
nuclear power is emerging as a strategic long-term option for hyperscalers seeking
stable baseload supply. Solar and wind can add capacity and support decarbonization
goals, but grid reliability and storage become more important as power demand rises.
That supports investment in batteries, backup power and grid-scale storage, with lithium

and graphite among the relevant inputs.
The buildout also increases demand for industrial metals. Copper is a clear beneficiary
because it is essential to transmission, transformers, cooling systems and data-center
wiring. Aluminum and iron ore are used in facilities, electrical equipment, transmission
networks and supporting infrastructure. Semiconductor fabrication adds another layer
of demand through industrial gases such as helium and neon, high-purity quartz for

wafers and optics, and gallium and germanium for advanced chips, photonics, and
telecommunications. AI-enabled automation and robotics also depend on permanent
magnets that use rare earth elements, including neodymium-praseodymium and
dysprosium-terbium.
Demand has moved faster than supply: This demand surge resembles earlier cycles
in semiconductors and memory, where expectations pulled spending forward. Metals
and mining companies are already seeing earnings growth and margin expansion,

yet the industry has struggled to keep pace with demand. The imbalance matters
because commodity supply is slow to respond. Production of many strategic materials
is geographically concentrated, permitting timelines are lengthy, recycling capacity is
limited, and substitution can remain years from commercial viability. New mines and
processing facilities can take a decade or more to reach production.
Investment Implications
Commodities may increasingly serve

three portfolio purposes: inflation
protection, diversification from
traditional assets, and participation in
long-term infrastructure and resource-
demand growth, supporting a diversified
allocation across direct commodities
and select producer Equities rather than
a single directional trade.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Governments are also taking a larger role through industrial policy, export controls,
stockpiling and efforts to secure critical supply chains. Restrictions involving gallium,
germanium, graphite and other strategic materials underscore how geopolitics can affect
availability and pricing. As a result, the current cycle may produce greater scarcity and
pricing power than demand growth alone would suggest.

So far, this dynamic has been most visible in industrial metals, but the same forces could
extend into agriculture. Reshoring changes where food and inputs are produced and
processed, while inventory rebuilding can lift demand for fertilizer, fuel, machinery and
storage. Agriculture has not yet experienced the same earnings and pricing impact as
metals, but supply-chain resilience could make it a broader beneficiary over time.
Cyclical forces still matter: The structural thesis does not eliminate near-term

volatility. Energy remains a key transmission mechanism for inflation because oil and
natural gas underpin transportation, manufacturing and industrial production. Geopolitical
disruptions can cascade through supply chains, raising costs and moving both commodity
futures and spot prices. Currency dynamics matter as well. A stronger U.S. dollar can
restrain commodity prices by increasing costs for non-U.S. buyers, while renewed dollar
weakness could support emerging-market demand and reprice dollar-denominated

assets.
A stabilization of geopolitical tensions could create a more constructive cyclical backdrop
through lower energy volatility, improved business confidence and stronger capital
investment. Conversely, renewed conflict can produce sharp and uneven moves across
the complex. These crosscurrents reinforce the value of treating commodities as a
diversified allocation rather than a single directional trade.
Gold, implementation and the portfolio case: Gold is fundamentally different from

most industrial commodities, but it deserves attention. Its traditional relationships with
interest rates and the dollar have been less reliable lately. Even so, sustained central bank
and exchange-traded fund buying points to durable long-term demand despite bouts of
volatility such as the selloff earlier this year. Gold also tended to move inversely with oil
during the height of the Iran conflict, illustrating how different commodities can respond

differently to the same geopolitical shock.
Investors can access the theme through commodity futures or producer Equities. Direct
commodity exposure is generally the clearer vehicle for inflation protection, scarcity, and
geopolitical supply shocks. Producer equities can offer greater upside when commodity
prices rise faster than operating costs because earnings and margins expand, but that
leverage is asymmetric. Company-specific execution, financing, cost and valuation risks

can still cause Equities to underperform the underlying commodity. A blended approach
may capture both physical scarcity and corporate operating leverage.
Ultimately, commodities remain sensitive to energy markets, currencies, growth, and
geopolitics, but their portfolio role is expanding. They can provide inflation protection and
diversification while offering exposure to AI infrastructure, electrification, energy security,
defense modernization, reshoring and, potentially, agricultural supply-chain investment.

The strongest beneficiaries of the AI trade may therefore extend well beyond technology
companies to the materials and power systems that make deployment possible.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Rising Global Interest Rates and International Markets
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Strong earnings growth has so far delivered a fourth consecutive year of double-digit
gains for global Equity indexes in 2026, propelling them to new all-time highs. But a
growing risk throughout the year has been the increase in government bond yields, which

have also broken out to multi-decade highs across a number of the major markets. The
U.S. Treasury will this week begin its expanded Treasury securities buyback program
announced in mid-August in response to rising rates. But 10-year yields across all
the major G10 countries have moved higher, in many cases by as much as or more
than in the U.S. market. Against a backdrop of above-trend growth in nominal gross
domestic product (GDP), high energy prices and elevated inflation, investors appear to

be reassessing monetary policy expectations, particularly in Europe where most of the
2026 increase in 10-year yields has been driven by inflation breakevens (Exhibit 1). Fixed
Income markets are consequently looking for policy rate hikes from the European Central
Bank later this week and potentially from the Bank of Japan later next month.
The rise in global interest rates has also come alongside an expansion in fiscal policy
across the major markets. In Europe, 2026 and 2027 are expected to be the peak

spending years for defense and infrastructure expenditure plans unveiled last year. The
so-called Readiness 2030 package allocates €800 billion to increased European Union
defense spending as it looks to move toward its 5% of GDP target by 2035. And at the
same time, the German government relaxation of its national debt brake has directed
€500 billion toward funding for investment in clean energy, transportation and digital
infrastructure. Similarly in Japan, the government supermajority is looking to enact a

new stimulus aimed at raising investment levels across 17 strategic sectors such as
semiconductors, defense and shipbuilding, in addition to a planned two-year cut to the
consumption tax on food from April 2027. And in the UK, the new central government
leadership is also likely to announce new spending measures when its first national
budget is released next month. To the extent that increased public investment spending
can lift productivity and support nominal GDP growth in Europe and Japan, the rise in

yields need not undermine economic growth or derail the equity market advance. But
higher rates, especially in response to supply-driven energy price inflation, nonetheless
remain a risk for developed market equity returns going into the end of the year.
Exhibit 1: Global Bond Yields in Major Markets have Risen by as Much or More
than U.S. Yields in 2026.

For many emerging markets the picture looks quite different. In contrast with the U.S.,
Japan and Europe, inflation rates are generally well below central bank targets outside
a limited number of resource exporters in Latin America and the booming AI-linked
economies of Korea and Taiwan (Exhibit 2). As a result, policy rate expectations over

the coming quarters are stable or lower for several emerging markets (including China,
Malaysia, Thailand, Poland, Brazil and Mexico) according to Bloomberg consensus forecasts.
Investment Implications
Ongoing strength in economic growth
and corporate earnings should support
global equity markets even in the face
of rising rates. For Emerging Markets
(EM) Equities in particular, contained
inflation, flat to lower local policy rate
expectations and stronger fundamentals

should limit the impact. And we
continue to favor EM Equities over
International Developed Markets, where
inflation levels are generally still above
target, central banks are expected
to raise rates and investors will be
watching for fiscal sustainability as
governments implement expansionary
budget plans.

Historically, rising U.S. interest rates have been a major headwind for emerging markets.
But looking ahead in the current cycle, we still expect the impact of potential Federal
Reserve (Fed) rate hikes to be lower than in the past. Over recent years, the association
between tighter Fed policy and weaker EM returns appears to have diminished. EM
Equities for example were resilient in the face of aggressive U.S. rate increases in 2022
and 2023, broadly keeping pace with the rest of the world. This is in sharp contrast to

prior cycles in which tighter Fed policy has resulted in capital flight, deleveraging and
falling asset values across the emerging world. Particularly during the crisis periods of
the 1980s and 1990s—but also more recently during the 2015 taper tantrum—U.S.
monetary tightening has tended to attract capital away from riskier emerging countries
(particularly those with large current account deficits), reducing support for local asset
prices and exchange rates.

Associated strength in the U.S. dollar has typically been an additional hurdle. For those
emerging economies with fixed exchange rates, a loss of export competitiveness has
further undermined current account positions, forcing central banks to run down their
foreign exchange reserves. And where exchange rates are floating, foreign currency
debts have risen in local currency terms, resulting in widespread defaults, debt
downgrades, recessions and additional exchange rate depreciation.

But starting in the years that followed the 1997-1998 Asian financial crisis and
continuing into the current cycle, the fundamentals across much of the EM universe have
improved. Fixed exchange rates have been abandoned. EM foreign currency reserves have
roughly doubled as a share of GDP . The share of EM debt denominated in U.S. dollars
has fallen significantly, led by the private sector across households and corporates. And
for most countries, current account deficits have narrowed or disappeared entirely. As a

result, emerging markets have become much more insulated from the direction of U.S.
interest rates on a structural basis, and this has only been reinforced by the AI-driven
revaluation of key Asian markets in 2026. Greater concentration in Korea and Taiwan has
increased the EM weighting in the surplus Asia-Pacific region to 83% as at the end of
August—up from 81% at the start of the year and 71% a decade ago.
Above-trend growth in nominal GDP and ongoing strength in corporate earnings should

support global equity markets even in the face of rising interest rates. But for emerging
Equities in particular, contained inflation relative to central bank targets, flat to lower
local policy rate expectations and stronger economic fundamentals should limit the
impact from higher global rates. We continue to favor EM Equities over International
Developed Markets, where inflation levels are generally still above target, central banks
are expected to raise interest rates and investors will be watching for fiscal sustainability

as governments implement expansionary budget plans.

### Page 6

6 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The One-Offs Inflating Q2 Earnings
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Headline Q2 earnings numbers look almost too good to be true. And in a sense, they are.
With nearly all of S&P 500 companies’ results reported, EPS growth is tracking around
50% year-over-year (YoY)—not just an acceleration from Q1 but a second consecutive
quarter above 20%. Chiefly skewing the headline number are massive mark-to-market

gains on private investments for a handful of mega cap technology companies like
Alphabet and Amazon that booked outsized non-operating gains tied to their stakes in
Anthropic, while Microsoft benefited from its OpenAI investment. Strip those out, and
adjusted EPS growth notches down to 31%. The point being, after being sliced and
diced, 30%+ is well above any long-run historical earnings growth rate.
The one-off nature of earnings strength wasn’t limited to investment gains. A second,

less-discussed boost was tariff refunds. As Exhibit 3 illustrates, revenues from tariffs
steadily climbed from early 2025 through mid-2026 until the International Emergency
Economic Powers Act (IEEPA) tariff ruling which initiated the refund process. As
refunds were disbursed, customs receipts turned net negative in June and July, with
refund payments exceeding total tariff collections. Customs and Border Protection cash
withdrawals of more than $100 billion in refunds have now been paid—60% of the $165

billion in total eligible claims.
The recipients: the “importers of record,” or the dozens of S&P 500 companies that
have disclosed billions in refunds over the past two quarters; Apple alone received $2.19
billion, adding $0.11 to per-share earnings to their quarter. Other disclosed refunds
include Nike with $986 million, Amazon with $640 million, and General Motors refunded
$500 million (although accounting treatment and use of proceeds has varied).

The boost of investment gains and/or refunds may not be repeatable in future quarters.
Perhaps more telling is that the median company’s revenue grew roughly 15% YoY, the
strongest gain in years, corroborating that there is operating momentum rather than
just an accounting mirage for Q2 earnings. Margins, too, as a percent of GDP are at
their highest back to 1950, hovering north of 22%, see Exhibit 4. That this is occurring
amid rising rates and at a time of geopolitical uncertainty/disruption, rather than a post-

recessionary recovery, makes the results that much more impressive.
The Path Forward: Questions remain about the sustainability of these elevated growth
rates, although consensus expects EPS growth to stay above 20% in both Q3 and Q4.
If achieved, every quarter this year would see 20%-plus EPS growth. On the back of
brighter outlooks from Corporate America in recent weeks, full-year earnings growth
will decelerate into the mid-teens in 2027, but that moderation comes off a high base

of 2026. For now, the breadth of earnings growth is the encouraging story as 10 of
the 11 sectors reported growth (Energy and Communication Services leading the way,
Healthcare the only detractor). That’s a reflection of strong core businesses growing
revenues into 2027’s tougher YoY comparisons.
Investment Implications
Underlying earnings-per-share (EPS)
growth of around 30%, stripped
of investment gains, is historically
exceptional. It remains that robust

corporate earnings and positive
revisions have helped Equities absorb
concerns around the recent rise
in yields.

Document 242: Europe’s search for a new growth model

Source PDF: all report pdf/Europe’s search for a new growth model.pdf

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article  |  3 September 2026
Europe’s search for a new growth model
The external environment that sustained the eurozone s export-led
model for decades has changed for good. This means that the growth
engine can now rebalance towards domestic demand or benefit from a
reinvented export edge. Neither comes with a clear-cut playbook, and
both can go horribly wrong
Rotterdam port in the Netherlands is the largest port in Europe

Why change a winning team?
Europe's economic model worked so well for so long that changing it never felt particularly
urgent. For decades, Europe was one of the biggest beneficiaries of globalisation. Trade
became an ever-larger part of the economy, companies gained access to fast-growing
markets abroad, and consumers benefited from cheap imports.
Calls for more innovation, higher productivity and stronger competitiveness never disappeared,
but they rarely moved beyond strategy papers and political ambitions. Why change a winning

team? The Lisbon Strategy of 2000, which promised to make Europe “the most competitive
and dynamic knowledge-based economy in the world” by 2010, is a useful reminder of how
long Europe has been discussing today's challenges. And of how little discussion alone
achieves.
The discussion has since become even more pressing. The Russian invasion of Ukraine and the
energy crisis have pushed up production costs, while Chinese firms have become increasingly

competitive in industries that Europe once regarded as its natural strengths. At the same time,

### Page 2

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 3 September 2026 2
global trade has become less predictable and more politicised.
The effects are increasingly visible in trade flows. Since 2019, eurozone imports from China
have grown by around 50% while exports to China have declined, widening the eurozone
goods trade deficit with China by more than €150bn. But bilateral trade balances should not be
confused with competitiveness. To assess competitiveness, it is more useful to look at market

shares. Here too, the picture has deteriorated. Europe's traditional strength in machinery and
transport equipment has weakened, with trade surpluses shrinking and Chinese firms gaining
ground in sectors such as electric vehicles. Even pharmaceuticals, the undisputed bright spot
in European trade with exports up more than 80% since 2019, are not immune to growing
Chinese competition, as China's share of eurozone imports continues to rise.
A trade surplus that once seemed entrenched is being eroded by several sides at once. And

while a trade surplus is not necessarily a sign of economic strength or something to pursue,
the shrinking surplus is illustrative of a deteriorating growth model characterised by
manufacturing specialisation, strong external demand, sustained export-market gains and
sizeable current-account surpluses. Subsidies, industrial policy and trade barriers may slow the
adjustment and buy time for businesses to adapt, but they are unlikely to recreate the

conditions of the 2010s.
This is what makes today's competitiveness debate different from earlier ones. While the war
in the Middle East and US tariffs have dominated headlines over the past year, they are only
one part of the story. The larger challenge is that the external environment on which Europe's
growth model relied has changed fundamentally. As a result, the eurozone is being pushed
towards a new growth model. The question is what that model will look like.

The engine is sputtering
The pressure on the trade balance stems mainly from the export side. The eurozone
has become less of an export powerhouse in recent years. To put this into perspective, the
contribution of gross exports to GDP was, on average, around two percentage points per
year in both the first and second half of the 2010s. Since 2020, that contribution has fallen
below one percentage point, and in Germany, of all places, it has turned slightly negative. The

Netherlands, the eurozone's most open economy, has seen the export contribution to growth
drop to a quarter of its pre-pandemic level.

### Page 3

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 3 September 2026 3
Contributions of gross exports to GDP have fallen this decade
Source: Eurostat, ING Research
The same picture emerges over a longer time horizon. Since the mid-1990s, eurozone exports
have outpaced GDP growth in every expansion and contracted more sharply than GDP in
downturns. Meanwhile, domestic demand as a share of GDP retreated after the financial and
euro crises and has never fully recovered. But in recent years, there has been a pickup. And in

Germany, this pickup seems more structural. The current expansion is the first in which export
growth has failed to outgrow the economy. Of course, there are temporary factors playing a
role here, but the structural story behind it is becoming harder to ignore.
The role of exports in GDP growth is moderating
Homemade growth
One possible response to a weakening export engine is a more domestically driven growth
model, in which the eurozone's large pool of relatively wealthy consumers and businesses as

well as fiscal stimulus become a larger source of growth through consumption and
investment. This is more than a theoretical possibility. The eurozone combines high household
wealth, substantial private savings and relatively low levels of household leverage with
significant investment needs in defence, infrastructure, energy and digitalisation. If these

### Page 4

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 3 September 2026 4
resources can be mobilised more effectively, domestic demand could become a stronger
source of growth than it has been for much of the past decade.
Recent initiatives could give such a shift additional momentum. Higher defence and
infrastructure spending, particularly in Germany, are boosting investment demand. The
Savings and Investments Union aims to channel Europe's large pool of savings towards

productive investment, while policymakers are increasingly focused on reducing barriers within
the single market. Together, these developments could provide stronger support for domestic
demand than Europe has seen in many years.
But how likely is it that domestic demand can simply take over? It’s a clear opportunity, but we
don’t want to close our eyes to the complications either. Below, we focus on three limiting
factors: the lack of historical precedents, the euro, and demographics.

The precedent problem
There are remarkably few examples of countries that have successfully moved from an export-
led to a domestically-driven growth model. The United States is the obvious one: a large trade
surplus at the end of World War II turned into the historically large deficits of the current era,
while the economy kept growing. The dollar’s reserve currency role did not cause America’s
shift towards persistent external deficits, but it made those deficits easier and cheaper to

finance than they would be for almost any other country. The US could rebalance on other
people's savings.
The other precedents are less flattering. Historical experience suggests that external
rebalancing is compatible with solid growth when it reflects higher productive investment, but
not when it is driven by credit-fuelled demand booms or declining competitiveness. Or worse,
often the so-called rebalancing happens in times of severe crisis. Finland's current account

surplus shrank sharply after 2008, but mainly because of the demise of Nokia and weaker
export performance; GDP growth fell from around 3% to essentially zero. That was rebalancing
out of weakness, not strength.
Spain, Portugal and Greece saw their external balances deteriorate after entering the
monetary union as cheap capital fuelled consumption and construction booms. Growth was
strong until the bubble burst. Too much of the capital flowed into housing and non-tradables

rather than productivity-enhancing investment, leaving these economies exposed when
financing conditions tightened after 2008. Ireland was, to a degree, in the same boat as
Southern Europe but has recently become more of a rebalancing success story. However, its
national accounts are so distorted by multinational activity that both GDP and current account
figures need to be taken with a large pinch of salt.
This means there is no blueprint for a highly developed economy to deliberately and

successfully transform its economic business model away from exports to more domestic

### Page 5

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 3 September 2026 5
demand. The eurozone would be entering uncharted territory.
Can a ‘global euro’ help build a new growth model?
As the US was able to use the dollar’s status as the world reserve currency to rebalance, could
the euro perhaps be of help as well? A successful rebalancing and a strong euro are best
understood as a feedback loop rather than a one-way street. If Europe's domestic investment

story becomes credible, foreign capital would flow in and the euro could strengthen. A
stronger euro, in turn, raises households' purchasing power, lowers import costs and shifts
relative incentives away from exporting and towards serving the home market – which is
precisely what a rebalancing requires. This is the mechanism that dollar strength provided for
the US for decades.
The loop, unfortunately, also runs in reverse. A weak euro flatters exporters, subsidises the old

model and postpones the adjustment – while making imports, not least energy, more
expensive for the domestic economy. European Central Bank President Christine Lagarde has
been promoting a “global euro moment”. And while the euro’s international role has increased
modestly (when measured at current exchange rates), it would be a stretch to call this a
material shift towards euro assets in global financial markets.
Without deeper and more liquid capital markets, a genuine European safe asset and a larger

pool of investable euro instruments, the euro will not get the reserve-currency tailwind that
carried the American rebalancing. In short: the currency will not lead this transition, but it is a
gauge of where the transition stands. Markets will reward a credible rebalancing with a
stronger euro, and punish an unconvincing effort by keeping the eurozone dependent on the
very export model it is trying to outgrow.
Ageing makes domestic demand growth more challenging

Demographics are the second complication, and they lean against the domestic-demand story
rather than for it. Eurozone population growth is grinding to a halt due to rapid ageing, and
ageing societies are not natural consumption machines. Without much population growth, it is
a lot harder to increase demand. On top of that, while the theory is ambiguous, we so far see
that older households save well into retirement, run down wealth more slowly than the

textbooks assume so far, and shift their spending towards services and pharma rather than
the goods and housing that drive investment cycles. The effects of ageing on labour supply,
potential growth and the composition of demand also make a domestically-led acceleration of
economic growth hard to achieve.
Japan is the main advanced market that has already gone through a sizeable demographic
decline and its growth model has become more reliant on exports over time. The period of

significant ageing of the Japanese population went hand in hand with a rising, not falling,
share of exports in GDP although this also went hand in hand with a prolonged balance sheet

### Page 6

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 3 September 2026 6
recession. As the domestic market matured and shrank, Japanese companies increasingly
looked abroad for growth, and the economy as a whole relied on earning income from the rest
of the world to fund retirement at home. If Europe's future relied on ageing consumers
suddenly discovering their inner spender, this is again something that hasn’t really been done
before.
In Japan, ageing has come with a rising share of exports in GDP

Source: Macrobond
A second lease for the export model
The lack of clear historical precedents suggests that Europe's way forward does not
necessarily lie in a rebalancing towards domestic demand alone. Another possibility is that
Europe retains an export-oriented growth model, not by defending yesterday's champions,
but by building tomorrow's. Europe remains one of the world's largest trading blocs, home to
global industrial leaders, deep engineering expertise and a large integrated market. If these

strengths can be translated into new competitive advantages, exports could continue to play a
central role in growth. Several candidates for these new export champions stand out. Defence
and aerospace benefit from a rearmament cycle that will run for a decade and from
procurement volumes that finally offer European scale; Airbus is the proof that Europe can
build a global champion when it pools resources. Pharmaceuticals and medical technology are

already the bright spot of European trade, and an ageing world is a growing market. Although
China is rapidly building a strong position in pharma at the moment as well.
In green technologies, the solar battle is lost and the EV battle is difficult, but grid technology,
wind services and nuclear expertise remain contested rather than conceded. Industrial AI and
robotics may be Europe's most underrated card. Europe will not out-compete the US or China

in AI models, but applying AI to the world's largest installed industrial base - the machine data

### Page 7

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 3 September 2026 7
of thousands of hidden champions - is a race that is still open. And services exports have
quietly become a larger share of the European story, from business services to tourism, with a
persistent surplus that rarely makes headlines.
Add to this the recently agreed trade agreements – Mercosur, India, Australia – which could
support export volumes, even if their effect on the trade balance is uncertain and likely to be

modest.
Still, none of these opportunities are guaranteed. European exporters face higher energy costs,
intensifying competition from China, fragmented capital markets and a single market that
remains incomplete in important areas. Creating new export champions is therefore not
simply a matter of identifying promising sectors. It requires the same ingredients that would
underpin a successful domestic-demand story: stronger productivity growth, deeper capital

markets, room for innovation and a business environment that allows firms to scale. Not
impossible as we argue here, but a lot of work to be done.
Four roads for the eurozone economy
Ultimately, the choice is not necessarily between one model and the other. A stronger
domestic economy also breeds more opportunities for European businesses to become global
export champions. By strengthening the foundations of the eurozone economy, Europe could
support both a more demand-driven growth model and a renewed export advantage. But

without a healthy foundation, weaker versions of both could be a realistic outcome as well.
To illustrate this, we define four stylised scenarios for the eurozone as it grapples with the
erosion of its old growth model. In these scenarios, the key uncertainty is not whether Europe
becomes more dependent on domestic demand or continues to rely on exports. It is whether
Europe can generate sufficient productivity growth and new competitive advantages to

support either model. A balanced economy without productivity growth risks stagnation; an
export economy without new areas of competitiveness risks decline.
1. Prosperity at home – Europe finds a new growth engine in its own market
Europe deliberately shifts away from dependence on external demand and develops a
stronger internal growth model. Initiatives to strengthen domestic demand through public and
private investment succeed. A more aggressive shift towards renewables and nuclear power

reduces energy dependence significantly, and structural reforms unlock activity. Supported by

### Page 8

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 3 September 2026 8
the savings and investment union, Europe becomes more attractive for foreign investors;
capital inflows strengthen the euro. The wage moderation efforts of the past decades are not
repeated as labour becomes more expensive.
Together with the stronger euro, this reinforces purchasing power. Trade surpluses narrow
almost automatically – a symptom of success, not failure. Europe imposes import restrictions

on Chinese products but keeps the European market open for Chinese investment. While
Europe remains dependent on US AI providers, the smart application of AI lifts productivity,
and services become a larger share of European growth.
2. Stagnant Europe – rebalancing without renewal
Exports lose momentum and trade surpluses disappear, but Europe fails to generate sufficient
domestic dynamism to compensate. Higher commodity prices weigh on purchasing power

and ageing and weak productivity dominate. As European exporters lose market share and
exports cease to be the main growth driver, the European growth model does indeed become
more balanced – but it is a balance of the race-to-the-bottom kind. Productivity remains weak,
companies increasingly invest abroad, and the current account balances not because
domestic demand rises but because there is less and less to export. Rebalancing by default
rather than by design: the Finnish experience at a larger scale.

3. Industrial renaissance – Europe creates the next generation of export champions
Europe retains an export-orientated model, but not by defending existing industries. Reforms
in the home market allow for better scaled companies. The basis for revived growth is similar
to our ‘Prosperity at home’ scenario. New, globally competitive sectors emerge and sustain
strong external demand: defence, aerospace, renewable technologies and pharma, among
others. Industrial AI applications and robotics keep European companies at the forefront of

global industry, converting the world's largest installed manufacturing base into a productivity
advantage. Labour costs are kept under control by wage moderation and technological
advancements. The model stays recognisably European – open, trade-oriented, engineering-
heavy – but with a new generation of products under the hood.
4. Disneyland Europe – fighting yesterday's battle
Europe doubles down on preserving existing industrial structures through subsidies,

protectionism and wage restraint. The export model remains the ambition, but
competitiveness continues to weaken as structural reforms stay superficial and half-hearted.
Regulation continues to dominate AI implementation, keeping potential productivity gains low.
This is the worst-case scenario: a continued, gradual deterioration of prosperity in which
Europe's most reliable growth industry becomes showing tourists from Asia and America what
a great economy once looked like. Culture and history, after all, are harder to ruin than

economic prosperity.

### Page 9

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 3 September 2026 9
Le roi est mort – but where is the heir?
The old European growth model will not be voted out of office; it is being retired by the world
around it. The real choice facing Europe is therefore not between a domestic-led and an
export-led model. It is between deliberate change and change by default. Both ‘Prosperity at
home’ and ‘Industrial renaissance’ are within reach, and they are not even mutually exclusive

– a stronger home market and new export champions could happily coexist. Even if historical
evidence clearly suggests that an ‘industrial renaissance’ would be the model with a higher
chance of success.
In any case, both outcomes require the same clear but also disruptive policy choices, in order
to achieve productivity growth, cheaper energy, deeper capital markets and reforms that
survive contact with national politics. In fact, Europe won’t need to choose a new business

Document 305: OpenAI高管：Astra需求太猛，新的Pro订阅或被迫暂停

Source PDF: all report pdf/OpenAI高管：Astra需求太猛，新的Pro订阅或被迫暂停.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-09
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

OpenAI 产品负责⼈ Tibo 表⽰，对 Astra 的需求真的是史⽆前例的。优先事项始终是为现有
⽤⼾保持优质服务，但如果这种情况持续下去，我们可能不得不暂时暂停新的 Pro 订阅。
摘要：
GPT-6 Astra 的发布正在引发⼀场算⼒与容量危机。 OpenAI 核⼼产品负责⼈ Tibo 公开⽰警：
Astra 的⽤⼾需求已超出历史经验，公司可能不得不暂停新的 Pro 订阅。
Tibo 在 X 平台发⽂称， “Astra 的需求真的是前所未有。我们已经在拉动所有能拉的杠杆来维持服
务，但直到现在，我从未⻅过任何类似的情况 —— ⽽我们此前已经经历过⾮常陡峭的增⻓。 ” 他表
⽰，⾸要原则是确保现有⽤⼾的服务质量，但如果需求持续， “ 我们可能不得不暂时暂停新的 Pro
订阅 ” 。这是 OpenAI ⾼管⾸次就单⼀模型需求公开发出供给预警。
市场影响不⽌于此。摩根⼠丹利在 9 ⽉ 7 ⽇的报告中指出，GPT-6 Astra 的重要性在于它将 AI 的瓶
颈叙事，从 “ 已知需求需要多少基础设施 ” 扭转为 “ 随着模型智能提升，有多少新⼯作负载将变得

经济上可⾏ ”—— 前者是需求侧质疑，后者是供给侧压⼒。这对算⼒、电⼒和半导体产业链的定
价逻辑均构成直接影响。
Tibo 是谁：从 “ 额度之神 ” 到 OpenAI 核⼼产品掌舵⼈
Tibo 全名 Thibault Sottiaux ， 2024 年从 Google DeepMind 加⼊ OpenAI 。在 DeepMind 的六年间，
他参与搭建了⽀撑 AlphaGo 、 AlphaStar 等项⽬的基础设施，并在离职前短暂领导了 Gemini 的⼈
类数据团队。
加⼊ OpenAI 后，他迅速从内部⼯具开发转向⾯向⽤⼾的产品。他率领⼀⽀最初仅 30 ⾄ 40 ⼈的团
队构建了 Codex ，并于 2025 年 5 ⽉将其公开发布。 Codex 活跃⽤⼾随后以每天百万级的速度增
⻓，⾄今年 7 ⽉已达 800 万，远早于此前预测的 9 ⽉⾥程碑。
2026 年 5 ⽉ OpenAI 内部⼤重组后， Tibo 的职责进⼀步扩⼤ ——ChatGPT 、 Codex 与开发者 API 三

条产品线全部合并，统⼀由他负责执⾏， Greg Brockman 负责整体产品战略。这意味着他掌管
的是⼀个⾯向近 10 亿⽤⼾的超级应⽤⼊⼝。
Tibo 在 X 平台上以频繁为⽤⼾重置 Codex 额度著称，被开发者社区戏称为 “ 赛博义⽗ ” 和 “ 掌管
Token ⽔⻰头的神 ” ，甚⾄出现了专⻔追踪其重置动态的⺠间⽹站 TiboGPT 。这种⾼度透明的公
开互动，已成为 Codex 产品体验的组成部分。
Astra 的能⼒跃升：从 Answer Engine 到 AI Agent
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
191 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
GPT-6 Astra 的发布标志着⼀次实质性的能⼒边界扩张。 OpenAI 明确描述其能⼒为：操作计算
机、浏览⽹⻚、使⽤软件、编写和运⾏代码、完成复杂⼯作流，并在科学、⽹络安全和专业领
域执⾏⻓程任务。
在 OSWorld 2.0 测试中 —— 该测试由 CMU 及斯坦福等团队提出，考察 AI Agent 在数字环境中完成
复杂任务的能⼒并与⼈类专家基线对⽐ ——Astra 得分 72.6% ，⾼于 GPT-5.6 Sol 的 65.7% ；完成
任务的模拟时间从约 75 分钟压缩⾄约 40 分钟。
这⼀变化的核⼼意义在于：AI 正在从 “ 回答得更好 ” ，⾛向 “ 成功把事情做完 ” 。AI 的⼯作模式由
此从 “ ⼈ →AI→ 答案 ” ，演变为 “ ⼈ →AI Agent→ 任务 → 执⾏ → 结果 ” 。 Tibo 本⼈在公开分享中透
露，他每天向 Codex 分配超过 100 个复杂任务，包括整理资源、检查值班轮换、分析发布计划
等，并使⽤ Codex ⽣成个性化的每⽇⼯作简报。
Token 需求的乘数效应：⼯作能⼒提升与消耗成正⽐
Astra 的能⼒跃升，将从根本上改变 AI 需求的测算逻辑。在 Answer Engine 时代， AI 需求可以近
似⽤ “ ⽤⼾数 ×ARPU” 衡量；但在 AI Agent 时代，这⼀公式已经失效。
Token 总需求可以粗略表达为： Agent 数量  × 每 Agent 任务数  × 每任务 Token 消耗。这三个变量
在 Astra 发布后均⾯临同步扩张的压⼒。
⾸先是 Agent 数量的增加。⼀个⼈可能同时运⾏个⼈助理、编程、研究、财务等多个 Agent ，企
业端则可能从数⼗个扩展⾄数千个。其次是单个 Agent 承接的任务复杂度上升 —— 模型越能完成
复杂任务，⽤⼾就越倾向于将更重、更⻓的⼯作交给 Agent 执⾏。第三是 Agent 从 “ 偶尔调⽤ ” 变
为 “ 持续运⾏ ” ：任务越复杂，推理链越⻓，上下⽂越⼤，⼯具调⽤越多， Token 消耗越⼤。
这意味着完全可能出现 Agent 数量增⻓ 20% 、但 Token 总需求增⻓ 300% 的情形。 Tibo 对 Astra 需
求的预警，与这⼀逻辑⾼度吻合：能⼒提升直接驱动消耗放⼤，两者呈正相关。

• Data centres, power, compute and private investment-grade financing is further
diversifying private capital from buyouts.
• But the risk of AI disruption, overbuild, and capital misallocation is a frequent risk

discussed by large, listed private capital managers
• Our spotlight section includes an analysis of AI-related overfinancing risk
• Still, the macro environment is now the top point of concern amongst PC managers, even
more so than the exit environment
• Private capital growth remains strong but is becoming increasingly K-shaped. Scale,
investment performance, origination capabilities and product breadth are driving
fundraising toward the largest platforms, while weaker managers and less differentiated

products face pressure.
• Fundraising, AUM and fee-related earnings generally reached record or near-record
levels, despite weak sponsor M&A, uneven exits and disruption in parts of private wealth
and software.
• There has also been a drop in the number of articles on private credit in the media,
especially when it comes to “crisis”-mentioning variety. With public BDCs being at the
centre of this year’s rout, the drop in such trackable indicators likely bodes well for

sentiment and thus flows.
• The overall tone of Q2 results was strongly positive and confident, especially on
fundraising and structural growth. However, management teams were more cautious on
the timing of monetisations, private-wealth recovery, AI capacity risks and software
disruption.

### Page 7

Luke Templeman | Galina Pozdnyakova | Private capital monitor: AI is the 2nd biggest risk  |  September 2026
6
Key themes from Q2 earnings calls
Large, listed managers talk AI, wealth, fundraising, exits and credit
Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank.
1. AI infrastructure has become a firm-wide capital deployment and earnings theme, extending beyond data centres into power, compute, chips, connectivity and investment-
grade financing. Power availability is emerging as a more important constraint than capital. Although concentrated exposure to AI infrastructure is a risk if demand

expectations change, managers stressed contracted revenues, strong counterparties and hard-asset collateral as mitigants.
• Blackstone’s data-centre platform reached $185bn of total value, including projects under construction, up from $130bn at the start of 2026. Management said the
platform could double over the next few years and that the market for long-term ownership of stabilised data centres could eventually reach $1tn. The firm also said
nine of its ten largest Q2 mark-ups were AI-related.

• Apollo cited more than $8tn of cumulative AI-infrastructure investment through the cycle and led a $35bn Broadcom financing, described as the largest private-credit
financing to date.
• Brookfield estimated $10tn of AI-related capex across energy, data centres, compute and adjacent infrastructure. The firm said AI-related deployment was probably
running at approximately twice its normal 15%-20% business growth rate.
• Blue Owl reported more than 140 data centres owned or under construction and 15.3GW of leased and owned capacity

2. Private wealth remains strategically important, but private credit experienced a confidence and liquidity test. Early Q3 data suggests stabilising fundraising. The better wealth
outcomes increasingly came from private equity, infrastructure, real estate and multi-asset products rather than traditional private credit. Earnings calls suggest some
international family offices and smaller institutions behaved more cyclically than core US individual investors.

3. Record fundraising despite a "noisy" backdrop. Fundraising is concentrating with scaled, diversified platforms.
4. Realisations are recovering, but the market is highly selective (see pages 17-21 of this report)
5. Credit is broadening beyond sponsor-backed direct lending and real assets, infrastructure, defence and secondaries are benefiting from secular demand
6. Earnings are shifting toward recurring fees, transaction revenues and fee-related performance revenues. Firms are becoming more effective at capturing economics from

origination, financing and perpetual vehicles amidst a strategic shift from simply managing third-party capital toward monetising the broader platform through origination,
syndication, financing and advisory services.

### Page 8

Luke Templeman | Galina Pozdnyakova | Private capital monitor: AI is the 2nd biggest risk  |  September 2026
7
Key risks from Q2 earnings calls
Aside from AI, concerns are rising about rates and credit
Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank.
1. AI overbuild, capital misallocation and execution amongst most consistently voiced risks
• Brookfield said concerns about excess infrastructure capacity were reasonable and that, as in railways and fibre, some capita l would inevitably be poorly allocated.

• Blackstone acknowledged the potential for excessive exuberance, despite its conviction that power, chips and entitlements con strain supply.
• Both firms also identified scarce inputs and complex development requirements as execution bottlenecks.
2. AI disruption to software and white-collar services
• Blackstone said software, professional services and information services were experiencing weaker buyer interest despite many companies continuing to perform operationally.

• TPG reported mid-teens software bookings growth and said over 75% of its software exposure was well positioned to benefit from AI.
• Blue Owl said it had gained increasing comfort around software transition risk but continued to recognise it as a valid issue
3. Wealth-channel redemptions. Management frames this as narrative-driven, but a slower-than-expected sentiment recovery is a risk to retail flows. Managers emphasised that requests were
concentrated among a minority of investors concentrated in non-US family offices and smaller institutions and that underlying portfolio performance remained sound.

• The recovery appears to be under way but remains gradual. Blue Owl explicitly rejected a V -shaped recovery assumption, while Ares estimated that its BDC could return to flow stasis over the
next two to three quarters if current trends continued.
4. Realisation timing and reliance on capital markets. Managers stressed that timing is difficult to predict.
• TPG expects transaction and monitoring fees to step down in Q3 after pulling forward some transactions into Q2. Carlyle simil arly said Q2 capital-markets activity was unlikely to repeat in Q3

or Q4. Blackstone expects lower Q3 realisations before a stronger Q4 and 2027.
5. Credit-cycle normalisation and growing dispersion remain risks, although managers generally reported sound portfolio fundamental s in Q2.
6. Insurance competition, spread compression and regulation
• KKR said competition had increased and it was allocating somewhat less capital to insurance at current return levels. Managem ent described sector ROEs as structurally low in the current

competitive environment. Apollo cited 36 asset-manager entrants into retail annuities and argued that some competitors lack matu re origination, liability-generation and operating
capabilities. Apollo also highlighted offshore regulatory arbitrage and pushed for “equal capital for equal risk”, warning th at weaker regulatory structures could undermine industry trust.
7. Rates, inflation, energy and geopolitics. The macro backdrop remained a recurring source of uncertainty:

• Carlyle cited war in the Middle East, pressure on energy markets, stubborn inflation and AI -related public-market volatility.
• TPG said geopolitical uncertainty and changing rate expectations had reduced private -equity activity.
• Blackstone said higher rates were delaying the real -estate recovery and had pushed some exits out.

### Page 10

Luke Templeman | Galina Pozdnyakova | Private capital monitor: AI is the 2nd biggest risk  |  September 2026
9
Management comments – a few records in Q2 and a confident tone
Source: Company reports, Deutsche Bank.
• Stephen Schwarzman of Blackstone: “Blackstone delivered an outstanding second quarter, highlighted by strong growth in earnings and nearly $70 billion of inflows. Our
decision to lean into the artificial intelligence megatrend is leading to standout investment performance across numerous strategies and creating extraordinary opportunities for

growth. We’ve become a trusted partner at scale to many of the key innovators in this ecosystem, which positions our firm extremely well for the future.”
• Joseph Bae and Scott Nuttall of KKR: “Our second quarter results reflect the strength and breadth of KKR's global franchise. We reported record Fee Related Earnings, Total
Operating Earnings and Adjusted Net Income per share on both a quarterly and trailing twelve-month basis. Our continued return of capital to clients led to our strongest

monetization quarter ever and helped drive record new capital inflows over the past 12 months.”
• Marc Rowan of Apollo: “Our strong second quarter results reflect record earnings across Asset Management and Retirement Services, highlighting the quality and growing
scale of our business. We are at the forefront of modernizing how private markets operate by enhancing transparency, improving liquidity, and broadening access. In a market
evolving quickly with increasing demand for capital, the breadth of our origination capabilities combined with a principal mindset positions us to help shape what comes next.”

• Harvey Schwartz of Carlyle: “The second quarter was one of Carlyle’s strongest quarters in recent years, underscoring the power of our diversified platform. We delivered record
Fee Related Earnings, our highest Distributable Earnings in nearly four years, alongside strong fundraising, and exceptional realization activity. Carlyle continues to distinguish
itself as an industry leader and an outlier in returning capital to our clients, distributing nearly $7 billion during the quarter and $37 billion over the past year. …”

• Michael Arougheti of Ares: “We generated strong second-quarter results, including another record quarter of fundraising with more than $36 billion of inflows as our clients
continue to reward us due to our strong and consistent fund performance across our strategies. Our diverse global originationplatform enabled us to remain active investing in
attractive opportunities across the platform in a slower transaction environment and we are now seeing a meaningful pickup inour firmwide investment pipeline.”

• Jon Winkelried of TPG: “TPG delivered record results during the first half of 2026, reflecting the strength of our diversified and growing franchise…As clients increasingly
partner with TPG for access to our differentiated offerings across strategies, we continue to see significant opportunities to further expand our platform and build long-term
value for our shareholders.“
• Doug Ostrover and Marc Lipschultz of Blue Owl: “Blue Owl's ongoing growth in the second quarter reflects the strong investment performance we continue to generate across

strategies and the diversification of our business across platforms and geographies… The results we reported this morning continue to demonstrate the resilience of our
business and highlight our positioning as a key provider of scaled capital solutions in this evolving market landscape."

### Page 18

Luke Templeman | Galina Pozdnyakova | Private capital monitor: AI is the 2nd biggest risk  |  September 2026
17
Realisations 1/5
Reopening IPO markets, AI-related corporate demand and mature portfolios are all catalysts. But timing remains
unpredictable and exposed to geopolitics, public-market volatility, rates and AI-related valuation uncertainty.
Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Haver Analytics, Deutsche Bank.
1. KKR described Q2 as the largest monetisation quarter in its history, with realised

performance income of $848m and realised investment income of $220m.
Remaining unrealised gains were still approximately $18bn.
2. Carlyle returned nearly $7bn to investors in Q2 and $37bn over the prior year. Its
US buyout strategy returned 23% of fair value over 12 months, which
management said was more than twice the current industry average and above
the 20% long-term industry average.
3. TPG generated $5bn of Q2 realisations and almost $14bn in H1, up 28% year over

year.
4. Blackstone outlined the following segmentation when it comes to investor
demand for assets:
• AI, power, electrification and energy assets: strong strategic, sponsor and
IPO demand
• AI-unaffected businesses: broadly available liquidity
• Software, professional services and information services: materially weaker
activity because of AI uncertainty.
The firm estimated the third category represents approximately 30%-40% of the
private-equity market.

5. TPG similarly noted that industry realisations were down about 46% quarter over
quarter, although its own H1 activity was higher. It expects its monetisation
cadence to accelerate toward year-end and into 2027. Brookfield expects
transaction volumes to build in H2, while Blackstone expects weaker Q3
realisations followed by a stronger Q4 and 2027.

中國兩年期殖利率 1.25% 基於景氣下行壓力增，預估第三季有降準50bp空間。
新興市場股 中立 美伊談判持續，油價反覆震盪惟重返前高不易，新興最嚴峻通膨壓力緩解。
新亞強健基本面可望抵禦逆風，並正向看待重啟降息、能源自主的拉美。
新興亞洲 中立 AI長線趨勢續存，台灣與韓國已逐步顯現隨AI紅利外溢擴散至投資端的景
氣、企業獲利的正面挹注。此外可留意評價偏低且經濟有望上修的印度。

中國A 中立 中國景氣K型化加劇，投資方向維持選股不選市，聚焦受惠AI相關供應鏈
兼國產替代政策扶植的科技族群、稀缺原物料及高附加出口價值等產業。
香港 中立 留意權重股財報是否透漏削價競爭告尾聲，及資本支出轉為獲利進程結果。
台灣 正向 長線台股AI供應鏈握技術進程優勢與漲價潮，惟指數緩行，個股領跑。

重點摘要
3
• 極 端 氣 候、 補 貼 效 應 遞
減影響 ，除7月出口表現
佳外 ， 零 售 銷 售、 工業
生 產 與 固 定 資 產 投 資 均
較 前 月 下 降 並 大 幅 低 於
市場預期。
• 汽車出口增速60.4%，惟
中 國 境 內 汽 車 零 售 銷 售
衰退幅度續擴增至 -17%，
強 勁 的 汽 車 出 口 不 足 以
抵 銷 國 內 汽 車 銷 售 的 下
滑，汽車生產僅-0.1%。
• 一 線 城 市 房 市 景 氣 尚 穩
定 ， 但 二 三 線 城 市 銷 售
跟 不 上 庫 存， 房 市 景 氣
不容樂觀。
《投資關鍵-中國經濟K型化加劇》
中國7月經濟數據顯示景氣下行壓力擴大，外貿、政
策扶植相關產業優於內需。7月中國官方製造業採購

經理人指數由6月的50.3降至49.2，製造業商務活動
指數降至49.0。7月瑞霆狗PMI製造業、服務業PMI
也較前月回落至50.9、50.4。導因7月極端氣候、補
貼效應遞減影響，除7月出口年增速維持雙位數增長
23.9%優市場預期 ，零售銷售增速 0.6%、工業生產
增速4.5%與固定資產投資增速 -6.7%均較前月下降
並大幅低於市場預期。
7月細項來看，受惠人工智慧驅動的需求推動、高端
製造政策支持以及製造業尋求海外市場拓廣 ，積體
電路出口與產量年增率分別為 116.6%與20.7%較高
速成長 。汽車出口增速 60.4%，惟中國境內汽車零
售銷售衰退幅度續擴增至-17%，強勁的汽車出口不
足以抵銷國內汽車銷售的下滑 ，汽車生產僅 -0.1%。
以投資端來看 ，通訊 、電腦及其他電子設備年增
7.8%，也優於整體固定資產投資增速 -6.7%。房市
方面，新屋開工 、竣工和投資增速持續呈現雙位數
跌勢，觀察中古屋房價表現 ，一線城市房價月漲幅
略放緩至 0.23%，二、三線城市房價月增幅較前月

下滑，整體來看 ，一線城市房市景氣尚穩定 ，但二
三線城市銷售跟不上庫存，房市景氣不容樂觀。
高端製造出口、投資與生產表現遠優於內需
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/08。

重點摘要
4
• 韓國發布 2026 年 稅 制 改
革方案 ，預估未來 5年稅
收將增加 3.44兆韓元。稅
基改革並非為了提供大規
模的財政刺激 ，而是重新
分配稅收優惠的項目。
• 企業稅制的改革若順利推
行，有機會促使南韓企業
至今較低的股價淨值本因
資本市場改革計劃而有所
改善。
《投資關鍵-南韓改革啟動 ，稅制調整與投資並行 》
韓國3日發布2026年稅制改革方案 ，預估未來 5年
稅收將增加3.44兆韓元。稅基改革並非為了提供大
規模的財政刺激，而是重新分配稅收優惠的項目。
調整方案將綜合不動產稅收來源大幅增加至2.18兆
韓元，其次則是加值稅增加 6,007億韓元，所得稅
減少5,579億韓元， 法人稅減少1,636億韓元。

房地產的稅制調整為考量所持房產價值、非自住和
多套房綜合評估下，預計未來將被納入較高稅負級
距，市場解讀為「精準增稅」措施，雖僅影響約納
稅人 3.6% ， 但 卻負 擔 整 體 土地 綜合 不 動 產 稅的
78%。政府並規劃 2027~2028年間暫時調降地區
內多套房產的資本利得稅 ，並在2029年全面恢復
甚至收緊重稅標準，隱含驅動多屋族釋出房源。
另一個關注重點則在企業稅制的改革，過往韓國企
業的大股東若啟動繼承或贈與交易前後共四個月期
間，不排除有意圖維持低迷股價，因為可相應減少
稅負。然新稅制改革後涉嫌壓低股價公司，將拉長
達6.5年的估值計算期間 。倘若企業新稅制度施行
推算可能使課稅金額至少增加30%，同時並促使南
韓企業至今較低的股價淨值本因資本市場改革計劃
而有所改善。
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/08/18。

6月底啟動三大超級計畫後，韓國政府
加快推出相關配套措施，宣佈設立5兆
韓元半導體專項基金，重點投資芯片材
料、零組件企業以及無晶圓廠半導體企
業，並提供相關供應商總額5兆韓元貿
易融資。此外啟動規模1兆韓元為期10
年計畫，促進大企業和較小供應商合作
如半導體研發、測試及生產等領域。再
者也將促進超大型特殊產業園區法通過，
加快產業園區許可審批，提高大型半導
體項目建設落地速度。本行認為韓國政
府期鞏固產業發展競爭力，除存儲芯片
領域居主導地位外，強化半導體產業鏈
中較薄弱環節，並加快投資端繼出口、
消費外成為對經濟貢獻的推手之一。
重點摘要

在「實體人工智慧」的第二支柱下，韓
國政府的目標是將韓國打造為全球人工
智慧機器人領域的前三名。重點領域包
括：1）製造業轉型為人工智慧；2）保
障核心人工智慧技術與資料安全；3）
建立區域性大規模生產中心。在另一份
新聞稿中，韓國政府指出，將投資 20
兆韓元用於人工智慧製造業，目標是創
造 100 兆韓元的附加價值。
重點摘要
7
• 財報季開跑後 ，新興市場
獲利與中位數見上修 動能。
韓國第二季獲利年增速表現最佳，其次則是新興歐
洲、拉美市場，值得留意的是此次新興市場獲利中
位數也見上修。另一方面，輔新興市場的產業類別
觀察，實際第二季獲利增速優財報初期以韓國科技、
台灣科技與中國金融產業為主，而若扣除科技硬體
的獲利表現來看，第二季獲利增速也較財報公佈初

期時獲得上修，反應新興市場獲利從國家、產業皆
出現擴散與輪動的步伐。
• 觀察若扣除科技硬體的新

在「實體人工智慧」的第二支柱下，韓

重點摘要
8
• 台灣與韓國的景氣與財報
皆顯示科技出口紅利帶動
投資端的改善。
• 維持對台韓中長線正面的
投資看法不變 ，但建議嚴
控持股比重。
• 中國 7月經濟數據顯示景
氣K型化進一步加劇 ，市
場 大 多下 調3Q26經濟增
速預估。
• 中國股市投資邏輯維持選
股不選市 ，聚焦受惠 AI相
關供應鏈兼國產替代政策

扶植的科技族群 、稀缺原
物料及高附加出口價值等
產業，避開可能因內需不
振而拖累的景氣循環類股 。
歷經七月科技股股災後，隨最劇烈的籌碼面調整告
尾聲，八月北亞市場重啟反彈。台股重返中天期均
線之上，韓國股市有季線反壓關卡，上證股市尚待
挑戰年線壓力。從均線位置也反應基本面強弱差異，
台灣與韓國公布第二季經濟成長優市場預期，且觀
察科技出口紅利帶動進口、投資，最後循序往內需
動能擴散，形成經濟的正向循環，市場也相繼上修
對台灣與韓國 2026年的經濟成長預估 。而從財報
成績單觀察 ，不意外的是台灣與韓國科技 2Q26獲
利增速較財報季初期進一步提升，但觀察韓國工業
受惠海內外投資計畫，台灣金融股因投資加速放貸
等兒也出現獲利上調契機，產業輪動的投資機會正
在增加。本行維持對台韓中長線正面的投資看法不
變，但建議嚴控持股比重。
中國股市方面 ，7月經濟數據顯示景氣 K型化進一
步加劇，出口、高科技相關表現遠優於零售、投資、
房地產。政治局會議雖表示加快政策逆週期調節，
惟不排除由於官方宣布展開為期一年的新一輪地方

「掃黑除惡 」專項，景氣下行壓力續存 。7月經濟
數據公布以來 ，市場大多下調 3Q26經濟增速預估 ，
較樂觀者則因考量經濟下行壓力加大，推估官方將
加碼穩增長政策，因此上修4Q26、1Q27經濟增速。
因此綜合基本面與財報來看，本行中國股市投資邏
輯維持選股不選市，聚焦受惠AI相關供應鏈兼國產
替代政策扶植的科技族群、稀缺原物料及高附加出
口價值等產業，避開可能因內需不振而拖累的相關
景氣循環類股。

局，獲利擴散的順週期類股如金融 、工業，估值具
有優勢的防禦類股如健護 ，將可望受惠；科技內部
也開始輪動，資金不再只高度集中在AI硬體。

《貨幣政策-長債殖利率攀升，聯準會極欲證明自身
對通膨的決心》
美國8月公布的就業 (非農月減2.3萬人)、通膨(核心
CPI年增2.5%)、消費數據(零售銷售月減 0.6%)，均
指向聯準會無需急於升息，9月升息的機率一度降到
40%以下，帶動美國短天期公債殖利率下滑 ，然長
天期利率卻持續上揚。
8月底聯準會主席華許在 Jackson Hole發表演說 ，
他表示「目前的數據並沒有讓我相信 ，通膨趨勢已
經出現有意義的改善」、「2%是一個堅定；固定的
通膨目標」、「目前的利率與金融條件並不具有限
制性」。鷹派的發言，使9月升息的機率再度攀升至
50%左右。
9月是否升息 ，關鍵在於 9月時公布的 8月通膨數據 。
本行仍認為9月按兵不動的機率略高於升息，主因：

(1)綜合市場預估和先前趨勢推演 ，本行認為 8月核
心CPI月增率將落在 0.2%左右。若此，聯準會有理
由採取觀望。
(2)美國近期失業率呈現下行趨勢 ，但主要是因勞動
參與率同步下滑所致 ，新增就業人數其實相當溫和 ，
今年以來沒有任何一個月份新增達到 20萬人，7月
甚至月減2.3萬人。。
風險：近一年來通膨的壓力大多集中在能源和商品
類別，服務通膨表現溫和，若8月看到服務通膨數據
出現上行壓力 ，則聯準會將不得不採取行動已證明
其公信力。
重點摘要
• 長債殖利率攀升 ，聯準會極
欲證明自身對通膨的決心

《美股盤勢-獲利成長和利率壓力拉鋸，美股短線有
壓然無礙趨勢》
歷史經驗顯示 ，無風險利率上揚時期 ，股市情緒通
常受壓抑 ，然今年以來美 10年期利率自 4%上升至
4.7%，不時有對短線盤勢造成干擾 ，但並未明顯影
響趨勢，主要是因近年的特殊環境：在 AI的強力趨
勢面前，即使資金成本很高 ，大企業仍持續擴大投
資，且確實帶來獲利 ，獲利的成長大過估值的壓縮
幅度。標普500指數在2Q26的企業獲利年增率預估
將來到50%，雖然這是因大型科技公司將其私募投
資持股市價重估後產生的收益 ，但排除後，獲利年
增率仍達30%。另一方面，因為資本市場活絡 ，金
融流動性大多時間維持在寬鬆區間 ，並沒有因利率
上揚或聯準會的鷹派預期而進入緊縮。

因此，本行仍維持美股的正向看法 ，不過短線震盪
風險仍是加大 ，在財報季告一段落後 ，市場仍很可
能開始放大檢視利率上揚帶來的影響 ，尤其在市場
開始討論AI生態系投資與算力融資之際 。研判市場
資金將維持輪動格局 ，獲利擴散的順週期類股如金
融、工業，估值具有優勢的防禦類股如健護 ，將可
望受惠；科技內部也開始輪動 ，資金不再只高度集
中在AI硬體。
重點摘要
• 本行仍維持美股的正向看法 ，

《拉美股市-低估值搭配改革紅利，巴西選舉是關鍵
變數》
目前市場共識看多拉美股市的核心支柱是政策轉型 。
拉美許多國家正轉向放鬆監管 、提升企業投資誘因 、
強調財政紀律 ，而巴西大選將是重要的觀察點 。巴
西目前的總統魯拉的立場較為偏左 ，且不親美 ，他
的對手波索納洛會是較符合市場對於提升企業投資
誘因期待的選擇 。另外，在全球AI與資料中心快速
發展的關鍵時期 ，巴西有望在能源 、礦產、基礎建
設與數位經濟供應鏈中扮演更重要角色 ，加上巴西
擁有全球第二的稀土蘊藏量 ，目前因為煉製技術落
後因此開採能量有限 ，但在西方國家不願受制於中
國的環境下 ，若政權轉換 ，預計將會有更多的礦業
投資計畫在巴西展開。
新興市場的資金在2Q26主要往AI供應鏈走，拉美並

不受青睞 ，然近期資金已開始尋找 AI供應鏈外的價
值投資機會 ， 從估值角度來看 ， 拉美股市的
forward PE相對其他市場明顯低了不少 ，本行仍維
持拉美股市的正向看法。
不過估值要有明顯的擴張 ，需要政策方向更加可信 ，
財政更有紀律 ，讓過高的利率有辦法降下來 ，同樣
和巴西年底選舉後的政策走向息息相關。
重點摘要
• 看多拉美股市的核心支柱在
於政策轉型 。拉美許多國家
正轉向放鬆監管 、提升企業
投資誘因、強調財政紀律
• 拉美股市的 forward PE相對
其他市場明顯低了不少 ，低
估值本身提供了一定程度的
安全邊際 ，不過估值要有明
顯的擴張 ，需要政策方向更
加可信 ，財政更有紀律 ，讓
過高的利率有辦法降下來
5
